0<B_{1}<1
and all large
n
,

|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2}(a)-\sigma_{j}^{2}(a)|\leq 10n^{-\frac{1}{4}}+4B_{1}^{\frac{1}{8}},

(A.8)

where we use the facts that
n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\leq 1
and
n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\leq 1
for all
n\geq 1
and that
B_{1}^{\frac{1}{8}}\geq B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}\geq B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}
if
0<B_{1}<1
to simplify the upper bound.

With these bounds and (
A.5
), it holds that for all large
n
and
0<B_{1}<1
,
\varsigma\geq t|\sigma_{j}(a)-\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(a)|+|A_{1}|+|A_{2}|
on
\cap_{l=1}^{8}E_{l}
. Hence, we deduce that for all large
n
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}(a)\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(a)\big]^{-1}|\geq t)&\leq\mathbb{P}(\{|T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(a)\big]-\varsigma\}\cap(\cap_{l=1}^{8}E_{l}))+\sum_{l=1}^{8}\mathbb{P}(E_{l}^{c})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(a)\big]-\varsigma)+\sum_{l=1}^{8}\mathbb{P}(E_{l}^{c}).\end{split}

(A.9)

Next, we bound the probabilities of
\mathbb{P}(E_{1}^{c}),\dots,\mathbb{P}(E_{8}^{c})
, where
E^{c}
denotes the complementary event of an event
E
. By Markov’s inequality, Condition
1
, and that the training sample is an independent sample, for all
n\geq 1
and
B_{1}>0
,

\mathbb{P}(E_{1}^{c})\leq n^{\frac{1}{4}}B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(A.10)

To deal with
\mathbb{P}(E_{2}^{c})
, notice that
\{Q_{i},\sigma(\bm{X}_{i},\dots,\bm{X}_{1},\mathcal{X}_{0})\}_{i\geq 1}
with
Q_{i}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}\big)\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}
is a sequence of martingale differences, in which
\sigma(\cdot)
denotes the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the given random mappings, and
\mathcal{X}_{0}
denotes the independent training sample for training
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
. Then by Markov’s inequality, the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality
(
Burkholder
et al. 1972
)
inequality, Jensen’s inequality, the assumption of i.i.d. observations, Condition
1
, and that the training sample is an independent sample, there exists
C>0
such that for all
n\geq 1
and
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(E_{2}^{c})\\
&\leq 2B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}|n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}\big)\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}|\\
&\leq 2CB_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\sqrt{\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}\big)^{2}\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}\right)}\\
&\leq 2CB_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}\big)^{2}\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}\right)}\\
&\leq 2CB_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\big[m(\bm{X})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X})\big]^{2}}\\
&\leq 2CB_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big].\end{split}

(A.11)

By arguments similar to those used in (
A.11
),
j\not\in S^{*}
, and (
A.3
), there exists
C>0
such that for all
n\geq 1
and
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(E_{3}^{c})&\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\left|n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}\big)\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}\right|\\
&\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}C\mathbb{E}\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}\big)^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}}\\
&\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}C\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2},\\
\mathbb{P}(E_{8}^{c})&\leq n^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\left|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}})^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}-\sigma_{j}^{2}(a)\right|\\
&\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}C\mathbb{E}\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}})^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}-\sigma_{j}^{2}(a)\right\}^{2}}\\
&\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}C\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}})^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{4}-\sigma_{j}^{2}(a)\right\}^{2}}\\
&\leq C\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{8})}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}.\end{split}

(A.12)

By Markov’s inequality, Condition
2
, the assumption of i.i.d. observations, Condition
1
, and that the training sample is an independent sample,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(E_{4}^{c})&\leq B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}})^{2}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{4}\right|\\
&\leq B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X})-m(\bm{X})\big]^{4}\\
&\leq 4\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X})-m(\bm{X})\big]^{2}\\
&\leq 4\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(A.13)

By Markov’s inequality, Condition
2
, the assumptions that
\varepsilon
is independent of
\bm{X}
and that the observations are i.i.d., Condition
1
, and that the training sample is an independent sample, the following three inequalities (
A.14
) holds for all
n\geq 1
and
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(E_{5}^{c})&\leq 4B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left|\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X})-m(\bm{X})\big]^{3}\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\right|\\
&\leq 4B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\big[\mathbb{E}|\widehat{m}(\bm{X})-m(\bm{X})|^{3}\big]\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|\\
&\leq 8\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big](\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|)B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}},\\
\mathbb{P}(E_{6}^{c})&\leq 4B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{3}\mathbb{E}\big[|\widehat{m}(\bm{X})-m(\bm{X})||\varepsilon|^{3}\big]\\
&\leq 4B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{3}\big[\mathbb{E}(\widehat{m}(\bm{X})-m(\bm{X}))^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{3}\big)\\
&\leq 4\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{3}\big(\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{3}\big)B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}},\\
\mathbb{P}(E_{7}^{c})&\leq 6B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}})^{2}(\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i}))^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}\right|\\
&\leq 6\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(A.14)

By (
A.9
)–(
A.14
) and the model regularity assumptions, there exists some
N_{1}>0
such that for all
n\geq N_{1}
and each
0<B_{1}<1
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}(a)\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(a)\big]^{-1}|\geq t)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(a)\big]-\varsigma)+n^{\frac{1}{4}}B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n})^{2}(\frac{\log{n}}{2})(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}+B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}),\end{split}

(A.15)

where we simplify the upper bound as have done for (
A.8
).

To deal with the term
\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(a)\big]-\varsigma)
on the RHS of (
A.15
), we need the following results. By the Berry-Esseen inequality
(
Petrov 1977
)
,
\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})<\infty
,
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{8})<\infty
, (
A.4
), and that
\varepsilon
is an independent model error, there exists
C>0
such that

\begin{split}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{P}(T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)\big[\sigma_{j}(a)\big]^{-1}\leq x)-\Phi(x)|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}},\end{split}

(A.16)

which leads to

\begin{split}&|\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(a)\big]-\varsigma)-2\Phi(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(a)})|\\
&\leq|\mathbb{P}(T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)\leq-t\big[\sigma_{j}(a)\big]+\varsigma)-\Phi(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(a)})|\\
&\qquad+|\mathbb{P}(T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(a)\big]-\varsigma)-\Phi(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(a)})|\\
&\leq|\mathbb{P}(T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)\leq-t\big[\sigma_{j}(a)\big]+\varsigma)-\Phi(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(a)})|\\
&\qquad+|1-\mathbb{P}(T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)\leq t\big[\sigma_{j}(a)\big]-\varsigma)-1+\Phi(t-\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(a)})|\\
&\leq\frac{2C}{\sqrt{n}},\end{split}

(A.17)

where
C>0
is due to (
A.16
) and the second inequality holds because
T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)
is a continuous random variable. In addition, for each
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
,

|\Phi(x)-\Phi(x+y)|\leq 0.4\times|y|,

(A.18)

where
0.4
is an upper bound of the maximum value of the density of the standard Gaussian distribution.

We use (
A.4
), (
A.17
)–(
A.18
), and regularity assumptions to deduce that there exists some
N_{2}>0
such that for all
n\geq N_{2}
and each
t>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(a)\big]-\varsigma)\\
&\leq 2\Phi(-t)+2\times 0.4\times\varsigma\big\{c\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})\big\}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+2Cn^{-\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\leq 2\Phi(-t)+\varsigma\big\{c\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})\big\}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+2Cn^{-\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

(A.19)

which in combination with (
A.15
) and the fact that
n^{-\frac{1}{2}}=o\big\{(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n})^{2}(\log{n})(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}+B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}})\big\}
concludes the desired result of Theorem
1
for the feature index
j
.

Lastly, we note that the above arguments apply to each
j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
such that
j\not\in S^{*}
and Condition
3
holds with
(R,\kappa_{1})=(1,a)
, and that constants
N_{1}
,
N_{2}
above and
C
in (
A.16
) do not depend on feature index
j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
. Hence, we conclude the proof of Theorem
1
.

A.2
Proof of Theorem
2

The proof ideas for Theorem
2
are similar to those for the proofs of Theorem
1
: in the inequalities (
A.20
)–(
A.21
) below, we first separate the negligible terms from the statistic
\big[T_{\widehat{l}}(a)-\sqrt{n}\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]^{-1}
and then argue that the statistic
\big[T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]^{-1}
, which is the remaining term, is asymptotically standard normal because
\widehat{l}
is independent of
\{Y_{i},\bm{X}_{i},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{i},\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Recall that for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
,

\begin{split}T_{l}^{(\star)}(a)&=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}\big)\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2},\\
\sigma_{l}^{2}(a)&=\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in(-\infty,a]}\big]-\mu_{l}(a)\big\}^{2},\end{split}

and that expressions for
T_{l}(a)
’s have been given in (
A.1
). In addition, we have defined
\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})=\zeta_{0}
if
S^{*}=\emptyset
in Section
1.1
; the proof here applies to cases with empty or non-empty
S^{*}
. Note that if
S^{*}=\emptyset
, then
\mu_{l}(a)=0
for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
; see (
9
) after Lemma
1
and (
A.3
) in the proof of Theorem
1
for details.

Let us begin the formal proof of Theorem
2
. For each
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
n\geq 1
,
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}(a)-\sqrt{n}\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big\{T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\leq x\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]+|x||\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(a)-\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)|+Q_{1}+|Q_{2\widehat{l}}|\big\}\cap\{W(x,n,B_{1})\}^{c}\Big\}\\
&\hskip 17.00024pt+\mathbb{P}(W(x,n,B_{1})),\end{split}

(A.20)

where the event
W(x,n,B_{1})
is given such that for each
x\in\mathbb{R},n\geq 1,B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}W(x,n,B_{1})&=\Big\{|x||\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(a)-\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)|-Q_{1}-|Q_{2\widehat{l}}|>\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})\Big\},\end{split}

in which
\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})=|x|n^{-\beta_{2}}+\sqrt{n}B_{1}(-\log B_{1})+B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}
,
Q_{1}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}
, and

\begin{split}Q_{2l}=2n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}\big]\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\varepsilon_{i}\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\end{split}

for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
.

Similarly, we establish a probability lower bound. For each
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
n\geq 1
,
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big]\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}\\
&\geq\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big\{T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\leq x\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]-|x||\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(a)-\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)|-Q_{1}-|Q_{2\widehat{l}}|\big\}\cap\{W(x,n,B_{1})\}^{c}\Big\}.\end{split}

(A.21)

To analyze the RHS of (
A.20
)–(
A.21
), we need a uniform lower bound for the population variances. By Condition
3
for each
j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
with
(R,\kappa_{1})=(1,a)
, regularity assumptions, and Lemma
4
in Section
C.3
, there exists some
\underline{\sigma}>0
such that

\min_{1\leq l\leq p}\sigma_{l}^{2}(a)\geq\underline{\sigma}^{2}.

(A.22)

With event
W(x,n,B_{1})
and (
A.22
),

\begin{split}&\textnormal{ RHS of \eqref{theorem2b.3}}\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]^{-1}\leq x+\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})(\underline{\sigma}^{-1})\big\}+\mathbb{P}(W(x,n,B_{1})),\end{split}

(A.23)

and

\begin{split}&\textnormal{RHS of \eqref{theorem2b.6}}\\
&\geq\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]^{-1}\leq x-\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})(\underline{\sigma}^{-1})\big\}\cap\{W(x,n,B_{1})\}^{c}\Big\}\\
&\geq\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]^{-1}\leq x-\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})(\underline{\sigma}^{-1})\big\}-\mathbb{P}(W(x,n,B_{1})),\end{split}

(A.24)

where the respective first equalities in (
A.23
)–(
A.24
) follow from the definition of event
W(x,n,B_{1})
and (
A.22
), and the second inequality in (
A.24
) follows because
\mathbb{P}(A\cap B)=\mathbb{P}(A)-\mathbb{P}(A\cap B^{c})
for any events
A
and
B
.

In the following, we deal with the two terms on the RHS of (
A.23
)–(
A.24
), and begin with showing that
\mathbb{P}(W(x,n,B_{1}))
is negligible. In light of
\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{q\vee 8}<\infty
and Conditions
1
–
2
with
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}n^{\beta_{2}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}+\sqrt{n}B_{1}(-\log B_{1})+n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{4}{q-\beta}+\beta_{2}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}=0
, we show in Section
A.4.1
(the case here is a special case with
R=1
and
\kappa_{1}=a
there, and therefore the notation here is slightly different) that for each
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}&\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(W(x,n,B_{1}))=0.\end{split}

(A.25)

Next, we show that the distribution of
\big[T_{l}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{l}(a)\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{l}(a)\big]^{-1}
is asymptotically standard normal. By the Berry-Esseen inequality
(
Petrov 1977
)
, (
A.22
), the assumption of i.i.d. observations, and the assumptions that
\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{8}<\infty
and
\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})<\infty
, there exists some
C>0
such that for each
n\geq 1
and each
1\leq l\leq p
,

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{l}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{l}(a)\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{l}(a)\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}-\Phi(x)\big|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}.

(A.26)

By (
A.26
) and that
\widehat{l}
is independent of
T_{l}^{(\star)}
’s, it holds that

\begin{split}&\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{P}\big\{\big(T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big)\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}-\Phi(x)|\\
&=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|\sum_{l=1}^{p}\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{l}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{l}(a)\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{l}(a)\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}\times\mathbb{P}(\widehat{l}=l)-\Phi(x)|\\
&\leq\sum_{l=1}^{p}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{l}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{l}(a)\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{l}(a)\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}-\Phi(x)\Big|\times\mathbb{P}(\widehat{l}=l)\\
&\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}},\end{split}

(A.27)

where constant
C
is given in (
A.27
). In addition, for each
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}|\Phi(x)-\Phi(x+y)|\leq 0.4\times|y|,\end{split}

(A.28)

since the maximum value of density of the standard Gaussian distribution is less than
0.4
.

By (
A.22
), (
A.20
)–(
A.28
), there exists some constant
C>0
such that for all large
n
, each
x\in\mathbb{R}
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&|\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big]\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}-\Phi(x)|\\
&\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}+0.4\times\frac{\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})}{\underline{\sigma}}+\mathbb{P}(W(x,n,B_{1})),\end{split}

(A.29)

which in combination with the assumption
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sqrt{n}B_{1}(-\log B_{1})=0
(hence
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})=0
for each
x\in\mathbb{R}
) and (
A.25
) concludes the main desired result of Theorem
2
. For the other assertion of Theorem
2
, note that
\mu_{l}(a)=0
for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
when
S^{*}=\emptyset
. We have finished the proof of Theorem
2
.

A.3
Proof of Theorem
3

The proof idea for Theorem
3
is omitted because it is similar to that for Theorem
1
. Recall that we have defined
\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})=\zeta_{0}
if
S^{*}=\emptyset
in Section
1.1
; the proof here applies to cases with empty or non-empty
S^{*}
.

Let us begin the formal proof of Theorem
3
with (
A.30
) and (
A.31
) below. For every
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
,

\begin{split}T_{j}(\kappa_{r})&=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i})^{2}\\
&=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)(m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i}))^{2}\\
&\hskip 20.00003pt-2n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)(m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i}))\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\\
&\hskip 20.00003pt+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{2}\\
&\eqqcolon A_{1}(\kappa_{r})+A_{2}(\kappa_{r})+T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r}),\end{split}

(A.30)

where
T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}
and
\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i}=Y_{i}-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})
. In addition,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]^{-1}|\geq t)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]-\varsigma-t|\sigma_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})-\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})|-|A_{1}(\widehat{a}_{j})|-|A_{2}(\widehat{a}_{j})|+\varsigma),\end{split}

(A.31)

where
\varsigma=t(\log{n})(10n^{-\frac{1}{4}}+4B_{1}^{\frac{1}{8}})+n^{\frac{1}{4}}B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

Now, let us establish the bounds for
|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})-\sigma_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})|
,
|A_{1}(\widehat{a}_{j})|
, and
|A_{2}(\widehat{a}_{j})|
on the RHS of (
A.31
), and begin with the one for
|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})-\sigma_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})|
. By Condition
3
, the assumption that
\kappa_{1}<\dots<\kappa_{R}
, the equality (
A.32
) below

\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big\}=0

(A.32)

for each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
(which is due to
j\not\in S^{*}
; see (
9
) for details), and the assumption that
\varepsilon
is independent of
(\bm{X},\widetilde{X}_{j})
, it holds that for each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
,

\begin{split}\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})&=\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{4}\big\}\\
&\geq\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(\kappa_{r},\infty)\}}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{4}\big\}\\
&\geq c\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4}),\end{split}

(A.33)

where
c>0
is defined in Condition
3
. By (
A.33
), for each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
,

\begin{split}|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\kappa_{r})-\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})|&=\frac{|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})-\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})|}{\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\kappa_{r})+\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})}\\
&\leq\frac{|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})-\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})|}{\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})}\\
&\leq\big\{c\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})\big\}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})-\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})|.\end{split}

(A.34)

In addition, for each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
, we write

\begin{split}&\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})-\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})\\
&=-\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})-\big[\widehat{\mu}_{j}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{2}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]^{2}(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i})^{4}\\
&=\bigg\{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{\big[\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]^{2}\times\Big\{\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{4}\\
&\qquad\qquad-4\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{3}\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}+6\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad-4\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{3}\Big\}\Big\}\bigg\}-\big[\widehat{\mu}_{j}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{2}\\
&\qquad+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{\big[\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}-\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})\Big\},\end{split}

(A.35)

where
\widehat{\mu}_{j}(\kappa_{r})=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)\big(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i}\big)^{2}
, and define events
E_{1}(\kappa_{r}),\dots,E_{8}(\kappa_{r})
for each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
as follows.

\begin{split}E_{1}(\kappa_{r})&=\left\{\left|n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}\right|\leq n^{\frac{1}{4}}B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\},\\
E_{2}(\kappa_{r})&=\left\{\left|2n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\right|\leq B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\},\\
E_{3}(\kappa_{r})&=\left\{\left|n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}\right|\leq n^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\},\\
E_{4}(\kappa_{r})&=\left\{\left|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})^{2}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{4}\right|\leq B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\},\\
E_{5}(\kappa_{r})&=\left\{\left|4n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})^{2}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{3}\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\right|\leq B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\},\\
E_{6}(\kappa_{r})&=\left\{\left|4n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})^{2}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{3}\right|\leq B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}\right\},\\
E_{7}(\kappa_{r})&=\left\{\left|6n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})^{2}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}\right|\leq B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\},\\
E_{8}(\kappa_{r})&=\left\{\left|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}-\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})\big\}\right|\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\right\}.\end{split}

In light of (
A.35
) and these events, we now establish the upper bounds for
|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})-\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})|
,
|A_{1}(\kappa_{r})|
, and
|A_{2}(\kappa_{r})|
for each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
. On
\cap_{l=1}^{3}E_{l}(\kappa_{r})
, for all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

|\widehat{\mu}_{j}(\kappa_{r})|\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}+n^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

(A.36)

On
\cap_{l=1}^{2}E_{l}(\kappa_{r})
, for all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

|A_{1}(\kappa_{r})|+|A_{2}(\kappa_{r})|\leq n^{\frac{1}{4}}B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}.

By (
A.35
)–(
A.36
), on
\cap_{l=1}^{8}E_{l}(\kappa_{r})
, for all large
n
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, and each
0<B_{1}<1
,

|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})-\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})|\leq 10n^{-\frac{1}{4}}+4B_{1}^{\frac{1}{8}},

(A.37)

where we use the facts that
n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\leq 1
and
n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\leq 1
for all
n\geq 1
and that
B_{1}^{\frac{1}{8}}\geq B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}\geq B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}
if
0<B_{1}<1
to simplify the upper bound.

By these bounds, (
A.34
), the definition of
\varsigma
, and model regularity assumptions, it holds that for all large
n
, each
t>0
, each
0<B_{1}<1
, and each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
, on
\cap_{l=1}^{8}E_{l}(\kappa_{r})
,

\varsigma\geq t|\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})-\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\kappa_{r})|+|A_{1}(\kappa_{r})|+|A_{2}(\kappa_{r})|.

By this result and (
A.31
), we deduce that for all large
n
, each
t>0
, and each
0<B_{1}<1
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]^{-1}|\geq t)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\big\{\{|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]-\varsigma\}\cap\big\{\cap_{r=1}^{R}\cap_{l=1}^{8}\big[E_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big]\big\}\big\}+\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{l=1}^{8}\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[E_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{c}\big\}\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]-\varsigma)+\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{l=1}^{8}\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[E_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{c}\big\},\end{split}

(A.38)

where
E^{c}
denotes the complementary event of an event
E
.

Next, we bound the probabilities
\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[E_{1}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{c}\big\},\dots,\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[E_{8}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{c}\big\}
for each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
. Note that we have
|\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}|\leq 1
for each
1\leq i\leq n
and
1\leq r\leq R
in these events. By Markov’s inequality, the assumption that observations are i.i.d., the assumption that the training sample is an independent sample, and Condition
1
, it holds that for all
n\geq 1
,
B_{1}>0
, and
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
,

\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[E_{1}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{c}\big\}\leq n^{\frac{1}{4}}B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(A.39)

By the arguments similar to those for (
A.11
), Markov’s inequality, the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality
(
Burkholder
et al. 1972
)
inequality, Jensen’s inequality, the assumption of i.i.d. observations, the assumption that the training sample is an independent sample, and Condition
1
, there exists
C>0
such that for all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[E_{2}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{c}\big\}\\
&\leq 2B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\Big|n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\Big|\\
&\leq 2CB_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\sqrt{\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)^{2}\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}\right)}\\
&\leq 2CB_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left(n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)^{2}\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}\right)}\\
&\leq 2CB_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\big[m(\bm{X})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X})\big]^{2}}\\
&\leq 2CB_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big].\end{split}

(A.40)

By arguments similar to those for (
A.40
),
j\not\in S^{*}
, and (
A.32
), there exists
C>0
such that for all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[E_{3}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{c}\big\}\\
&\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\left|n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}\right|\\
&\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}C\mathbb{E}\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}}\\
&\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}C\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2},\\
&\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[E_{8}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{c}\big\}\\
&\leq n^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\left|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}-\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})\right\}\right|\\
&\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}C\mathbb{E}\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}-\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})\right\}^{2}}\\
&\leq n^{-\frac{1}{4}}C\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\{(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{4}-\sigma_{j}^{2}(\kappa_{r})\right\}^{2}}\\
&\leq C\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{8})}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}n^{-\frac{1}{4}}.\end{split}

(A.41)

By Markov’s inequality, the assumption of i.i.d. observations, the assumption that the training sample is an independent sample, and Conditions
1
–
2
, it holds that for all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[E_{4}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{c}\big\}&\leq B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})^{2}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{4}\right|\\
&\leq B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X})-m(\bm{X})\big]^{4}\\
&\leq 4\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X})-m(\bm{X})\big]^{2}\\
&\leq 4\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(A.42)

By Markov’s inequality, the assumptions that the observations are i.i.d. and that the training sample is an independent sample, model regularity assumptions, and Conditions
1
–
2
, the following three inequalities (
A.43
) holds for all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[E_{5}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{c}\big\}&\leq 4B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left|\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X})-m(\bm{X})\big]^{3}\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\right|\\
&\leq 4B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\big[\mathbb{E}|\widehat{m}(\bm{X})-m(\bm{X})|^{3}\big]\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|\\
&\leq 8\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big](\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|)B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}},\\
\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[E_{6}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{c}\big\}&\leq 4B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{3}\mathbb{E}\big[|\widehat{m}(\bm{X})-m(\bm{X})||\varepsilon|^{3}\big]\\
&\leq 4B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{3}\big[\mathbb{E}(\widehat{m}(\bm{X})-m(\bm{X}))^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{3}\big)\\
&\leq 4\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{3}\big(\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{3}\big)B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}},\\
\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[E_{7}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{c}\big\}&\leq 6\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(A.43)

By (
A.38
)–(
A.43
), model regularity assumptions, the assumption that
R
is a finite constant, and that
\varsigma=t(\log{n})(10n^{-\frac{1}{4}}+4B_{1}^{\frac{1}{8}})+n^{\frac{1}{4}}B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}
, there exists some
N_{1}>0
such that for all
n\geq N_{1}
, each
t>0
, and each
0<B_{1}<1
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]^{-1}|\geq t)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]-\varsigma)+Rn^{\frac{1}{4}}B_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}+(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n})^{2}(\frac{\log{n}}{2})(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}+B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}).\end{split}

(A.44)

To deal with the term
\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]-\varsigma)
on the RHS of (
A.44
), we need the following results. By the Berry-Esseen inequality
(
Petrov 1977
)
,
\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})<\infty
,
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{8})<\infty
, (
A.33
), and that
\varepsilon
is an independent model error, there exists
C>0
such that for each
n\geq 1
and each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
,

\begin{split}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{P}(T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})\big[\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{-1}\leq x)-\Phi(x)|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}},\end{split}

(A.45)

which leads to

\begin{split}&\left|\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]-\varsigma)-2\Phi\left(-t\right)\right|\\
&=\bigg|\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}\big\{\big\{|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})\big]-\varsigma\big\}\cap\{\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r}\}\big\}\\
&\qquad-\sum_{r=1}^{R}2\Phi\left(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})}\right)\mathbb{P}(\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r})\\
&\qquad+\sum_{r=1}^{R}2\Phi\left(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})}\right)\mathbb{P}(\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r})-2\Phi\left(-t\right)\bigg|\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r})\left|\mathbb{P}\big\{|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})\big]-\varsigma\big\}-2\Phi\left(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})}\right)\right|\\
&\qquad\qquad+2\sum_{r=1}^{R}\left|\Phi\left(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})}\right)-\Phi\left(-t\right)\right|\mathbb{P}(\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r})\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r})\bigg\{\left|\mathbb{P}(T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})\leq-t\big[\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})\big]+\varsigma)-\Phi\left(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})}\right)\right|\\
&\qquad\qquad+\left|\mathbb{P}(T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})\big]-\varsigma)-\Phi\left(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})}\right)\right|\bigg\}\\
&\qquad\qquad+2\sum_{r=1}^{R}\left|\Phi\left(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})}\right)-\Phi\left(-t\right)\right|\mathbb{P}(\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r})\\
&=\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r})\left|\mathbb{P}(T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})\leq-t\big[\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})\big]+\varsigma)-\Phi\left(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})}\right)\right|\\
&\qquad+\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r})\left|1-\mathbb{P}(T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})\leq t\big[\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})\big]-\varsigma)-1+\Phi\left(t-\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})}\right)\right|\\
&\qquad+2\sum_{r=1}^{R}\left|\Phi\left(-t+\frac{\varsigma}{\sigma_{j}(\kappa_{r})}\right)-\Phi\left(-t\right)\right|\mathbb{P}(\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r})\\
&\leq\frac{2C}{\sqrt{n}}+2\times 0.4\times\varsigma\times\big\{c\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})\big\}^{-\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

(A.46)

where
C>0
is given in (
A.45
); the first inequality is due to the assumption that
\widehat{a}_{j}
is independent of
T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})
’s; the second equality follows because
T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})
is a continuous random variable; and the last inequality is from (
A.18
), (
A.33
), and the fact that
\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r})=1
.

We use (
A.46
) and model regularity assumptions to deduce that there exists some
N_{2}>0
such that for all
n\geq N_{2}
, each
t>0
, and each
0<B_{1}<1
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})|\geq t\big[\sigma_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]-\varsigma)\leq 2\Phi(-t)+\varsigma\big\{c\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})\big\}^{-\frac{1}{2}}+2Cn^{-\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

(A.47)

which in combination with (
A.44
) and the fact that
n^{-\frac{1}{2}}=o\big\{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}(\log{n})(n^{-\frac{1}{4}}+B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}})\big\}
concludes the desired result of Theorem
3
for the
j
th coordinate.

Lastly, notice that generic constants in this proof as well as other statements of “for all large
n
” are not subject to feature index
j
(e.g.
N_{1}
in (
A.44
),
N_{2}
in (
A.47
), and
C
in (
A.45
)). Therefore, we conclude the proof of Theorem
3
.

A.4
Proof of Theorem
4

Before we begin the formal proof, we recall some notation for the reader’s convenience. We have assumed
\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})=\zeta_{0}
if
S^{*}=\emptyset
in Section
1.1
, and we have defined
G_{l}(\kappa_{r})=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{U_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})(\widehat{\eta}_{i})^{2}
with
\widehat{\eta}_{i}=V_{i}-\widehat{m}(\bm{U}_{i})
. The cumulative function of the standard normal distribution is denoted by
\Phi(\cdot)
. Change point candidates
\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{R}
with some finite
R>1
are such that
\kappa_{r}<\kappa_{r+1}
. In addition, we use the following notation for
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
and
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
.

\begin{split}\widehat{a}_{l}&=\arg\max_{\kappa\in\{\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{R}\}}|G_{l}(\kappa)|,\\
T_{l}(\kappa_{r})&=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i})^{2},\\
T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})&=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{2},\\
\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})&=\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big\},\\
\widehat{\mu}_{l}(\kappa_{r})&=\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big](\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i})^{2}\big\},\\
\sigma_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})&=\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{4}\big\}-\mu_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r}),\\
\widehat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})&=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big((\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i})^{2}-\widehat{\mu}_{l}(\kappa_{r})\Big)^{2}.\\
\end{split}

The proof ideas for Theorme
4
follow those for the proofs of Theorem
2
, and hence we omit the details. The formal proof of Theorem
4
begins with an upper bound and a lower bound of
\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big]\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}
as follows. For each
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
n\geq 1
,
B_{1}>0
,

{\small\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big]\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big\{T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\leq x\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]+|x||\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+Q_{1}+|Q_{2}(\widehat{l},\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|\big\}\cap\{W(x,b,B_{1})\}^{c}\Big\}+\mathbb{P}(W(x,b,B_{1})),\end{split}}

(A.48)

where the event
W(x,n,B_{1})
is defined such that for each
x\in\mathbb{R},n\geq 1,B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}W(x,n,B_{1})&=\Big\{|x||\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|+Q_{1}+|Q_{2}(\widehat{l},\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|>\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})\Big\},\end{split}

in which
\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})=|x|n^{-\beta_{2}}+\sqrt{n}B_{1}(-\log B_{1})+B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}
,
Q_{1}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}
, and for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}Q_{2}(l,b)=2n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,b]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,b]}\big]\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\varepsilon_{i}\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}}).\end{split}

Similarly, we establish the probability lower bound as follows.

{\small\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}\\
&\geq\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big\{T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\leq x\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]-|x||\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|-Q_{1}-|Q_{2}(\widehat{l},\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|\big\}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\cap\{W(x,n,B_{1})\}^{c}\Big\}.\end{split}}

(A.49)

To deal with the RHS of (
A.48
)–(
A.49
), we need a uniform lower bound for the population variances, which is given in (
A.50
) below. For every
x
with
\kappa_{1}\leq x\leq\kappa_{R}
and each
j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,x]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,x]\})&\geq\mathbb{P}(\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{1}]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{1}]\}),\\
\mathbb{P}(\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,x]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(x,\infty)\})&\geq\mathbb{P}(\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{1}]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(\kappa_{R},\infty)\}),\end{split}

which in combination with Condition
3
implies that for every
x
with
\kappa_{1}\leq x\leq\kappa_{R}
and each
j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,x]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,x]\})&>c,\\
\mathbb{P}(\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,x]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(x,\infty)\})&>c,\end{split}

in which
c>0
is defined in Condition
3
. By these inequalities, regularity assumptions, and Lemma
4
in Section
C.3
, there exists some
\underline{\sigma}>0
(see (
A.158
) in Lemma
4
) such that

\inf_{1\leq l\leq p,\kappa_{1}\leq x\leq\kappa_{R}}\sigma_{l}^{2}(x)\geq\underline{\sigma}^{2}.

(A.50)

By the definition of
W(x,n,B_{1})
and (
A.50
), the RHS of (
A.48
)–(
A.49
) can be further analyzed in (
A.51
)–(
A.52
) below, respectively.

\begin{split}\textnormal{RHS of \eqref{theorem4.31}}&\leq\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]^{-1}\leq x+\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})(\underline{\sigma}^{-1})\big\}\\
&\qquad+\mathbb{P}(W(x,n,B_{1})),\end{split}

(A.51)

and similarly,

\begin{split}&\textnormal{RHS of \eqref{theorem4.32}}\\
&\geq\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]^{-1}\leq x-\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})(\underline{\sigma}^{-1})\big\}\cap\{W(x,n,B_{1})\}^{c}\Big\}\\
&\geq\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]^{-1}\leq x-\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})(\underline{\sigma}^{-1})\big\}-\mathbb{P}(W(x,n,B_{1})),\end{split}

(A.52)

where the second inequality is due to the fact that
\mathbb{P}(A\cap B)=\mathbb{P}(A)-\mathbb{P}(A\cap B^{c})
for any events
A
and
B
.

Now, we proceed to show that the probability
\mathbb{P}(W(x,n,B_{1}))
on the RHS of (
A.51
)–(
A.52
) is negligible, and that
\big[T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]^{-1}
is asymptotically standard normal. In light of
\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{q\vee 8}<\infty
and Conditions
1
–
2
with the assumptions
\beta_{2}<\frac{1}{4}
and

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}n^{\beta_{2}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}+\sqrt{n}B_{1}(-\log{B_{1}})+n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{4}{q-\beta}+\beta_{2}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}=0,

we show in Section
A.4.1
that for each
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}&\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(W(x,n,B_{1}))=0.\end{split}

(A.53)

Next, by the Berry-Esseen inequality
(
Petrov 1977
)
, along with (
A.50
), the assumption of i.i.d. observations, and the assumptions that
\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{8}<\infty
and
\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})<\infty
, there exists some
C>0
such that for each
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq l\leq p
, and each
1\leq r\leq R
,

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\big|\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}-\Phi(x)\big|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}.

(A.54)

Furthermore, by (
A.54
) and that
\widehat{l}
and
\widehat{a}_{l}
’s are independent of
T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{1}),\dots,T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{R})
, it holds that

\begin{split}&\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}-\Phi(x)|\\
&=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\sum_{l=1}^{p}\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}\mathbb{P}(\{\widehat{l}=l\}\cap\{\widehat{a}_{l}=\kappa_{r}\})\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt-\Phi(x)\Big|\\
&\leq\sum_{l=1}^{p}\sum_{r=1}^{R}\Big\{\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big]\big]\big[\sigma_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}-\Phi(x)\Big|\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\times\mathbb{P}(\{\widehat{l}=l\}\cap\{\widehat{a}_{l}=\kappa_{r}\})\Big\}\\
&\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}},\end{split}

(A.55)

where constant
C
is given in (
A.54
). In addition, for each
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}|\Phi(x)-\Phi(x+y)|\leq 0.4\times|y|,\end{split}

(A.56)

since the maximum value of density of the standard Gaussian distribution is less than
0.4
.

By (
A.48
)–(
A.52
), (
A.54
)–(
A.56
), there exists some constant
C>0
such that for all large
n
, each
x\in\mathbb{R}
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&|\mathbb{P}\big\{\big[T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big]\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]^{-1}\leq x\big\}-\Phi(x)|\\
&\qquad\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}+0.4\times\frac{\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})}{\underline{\sigma}}+\mathbb{P}(W(x,n,B_{1})),\end{split}

(A.57)

which in combination with the assumption
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sqrt{n}B_{1}(-\log{B_{1}})=0
(hence
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\varsigma(x,n,B_{1})=0
for each
x\in\mathbb{R}
) and (
A.53
) concludes the main desired result. For the other assertion of Theorem
4
, note that
\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})=0
for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
and
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
when
S^{*}=\emptyset
. We have finished the proof of Theorem
4
.

A.4.1
Proof of (
A.53
)

First, we deduce that for each
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(W(x,n,B_{1}))\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\big\{|\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|>n^{-\beta_{2}}\big\}+\mathbb{P}\big\{Q_{1}>\sqrt{n}B_{1}(-\log B_{1})\big\}+\mathbb{P}\big\{|Q_{2}(\widehat{l},\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|>B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}\big\}.\end{split}

(A.58)

Recall that
Q_{1}=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}
and for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}Q_{2}(l,b)=2n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,b]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,b]}\big]\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\varepsilon_{i}\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}}).\end{split}

Below, we deal with the upper bounds for each term on the RHS of (
A.58
), and begin with the one for the second term
\mathbb{P}\big\{Q_{1}>\sqrt{n}B_{1}(-\log B_{1})\big\}
.

By Markov’s inequality, Condition
1
, the assumption of i.i.d. observations, and that the training sample is an independent sample, it holds that for all
n\geq 1
and
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(Q_{1}>\sqrt{n}B_{1}(-\log B_{1}))\leq(-\log B_{1})^{-1}.\end{split}

(A.59)

Next, for deriving an upper bound on the third term on the RHS of (
A.58
), we use the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality
(
Burkholder
et al. 1972
)
. To apply the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality, we have to specify the martingale difference sequence in
Q_{2}(\widehat{l},\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})
. Let
q_{2i}(l,a)=\big[\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}\big]\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\varepsilon_{i}\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})
for
1\leq i\leq n
and
q_{2i}(l,a)=0
otherwise. As a result,
Q_{2}(\widehat{l},\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{2q_{2i}(\widehat{l},\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})}{\sqrt{n}}
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{0}=\sigma(\mathcal{X}_{0},\mathcal{X}_{2})
and
\mathcal{F}_{i}=\sigma\big(\mathcal{X}_{0},\mathcal{X}_{2},\frac{2q_{21}(\widehat{l},\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})}{\sqrt{n}}\dots,\frac{2q_{2i}(\widehat{l},\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})}{\sqrt{n}}\big)
for each
i\geq 1
, where
\sigma(\cdot)
denotes the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the given random mappings,
\mathcal{X}_{0}
is the independent training sample for constructing
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
, and
\mathcal{X}_{2}=\{V_{i},\bm{U}_{i},\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
. Now,
\{\frac{2q_{2i}(\widehat{l},\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})}{\sqrt{n}},\mathcal{F}_{i}\}_{i\geq 0}
is a martingale difference sequence, and hence it holds that for some
K>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(|Q_{2}(\widehat{l},\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|>B_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}})&\leq B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}|Q_{2}(\widehat{l},\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|\\
&\leq B_{1}^{-\frac{1}{4}}K\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}}\\
&\leq KB_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z}),\end{split}

(A.60)

where the first inequality is due to Markov’s inequality; the second inequality is from
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})=1
, the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality, and Jensen’s inequality; and the third inequality follows from Condition
1
, the assumption of i.i.d. observations, and that the training sample is an independent sample.

Next, we proceed to establish an upper bound on the first term on the RHS of (
A.58
). Simple calculations show that for each
a\in\mathbb{R}
and
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
,

|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(a)-\sigma_{l}(a)|=\frac{|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}(a)-\sigma_{l}^{2}(a)|}{\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(a)+\sigma_{l}(a)}\leq\frac{|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}(a)-\sigma_{l}^{2}(a)|}{\sigma_{l}(a)}\leq\frac{|\widehat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}(a)-\sigma_{l}^{2}(a)|}{\underline{\sigma}},

(A.61)

where
\underline{\sigma}
is given in (
A.50
). In addition, for each
a\in\mathbb{R}
and
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
,

\begin{split}\widehat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}(a)&=-\big[\widehat{\mu}_{l}(a)\big]^{2}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,a]})^{2}(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i})^{4}\\
&=-\big[\widehat{\mu}_{l}(a)\big]^{2}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,a]})^{2}(m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i}))^{4}\\
&\qquad+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,a]})^{2}4(m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i}))^{3}\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\\
&\qquad+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,a]})^{2}6(m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i}))^{2}(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{2}\\
&\qquad+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,a]})^{2}4(m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i}))(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{3}\\
&\qquad+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,a]})^{2}(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{4}\\
&\eqqcolon-\big[\widehat{\mu}_{l}(a)\big]^{2}+(I)_{l,a}+(II)_{l,a}+(III)_{l,a}+(IV)_{l,a}+(V)_{l,a}.\end{split}

(A.62)

By (
A.61
)–(
A.62
), for each
t>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(|\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|>t)\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\sigma_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\widehat{\sigma}_{l}(\kappa_{r})|>t)\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\sigma_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})-\widehat{\sigma}_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})|>t\underline{\sigma})\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\bigg\{\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|(V)_{l,\kappa_{r}}-\big[\sigma_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})+\mu_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})\big]\Big|>\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\Big)\\
&\qquad+\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\widehat{\mu}_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})|>\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|(I)_{l,\kappa_{r}}|>\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\Big)\\
&\qquad+\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|(II)_{l,\kappa_{r}}|>\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\Big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|(III)_{l,\kappa_{r}}|>\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\Big)\\
&\qquad+\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|(IV)_{l,\kappa_{r}}|>\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\Big)\bigg\}.\end{split}

(A.63)

In the following, we give upper bounds on each term on the RHS of (
A.63
). By Markov’s inequality, the assumption of i.i.d. observations, the assumption that the training sample for
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
is an independent sample, and Conditions
1
–
2
, it holds that for all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, each
t>0
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|(I)_{l,\kappa_{r}}|>\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\Big)\leq\frac{24\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}}{t\underline{\sigma}},\end{split}

(A.64)

where we use the result that for each
1\leq i\leq n
and each
1\leq r\leq R
,

\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\left(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\right)^{2}\leq 1.

This result is also used for deriving the other probability upper bounds below.

By Markov’s inequality, the assumption of i.i.d. observations, the assumption that the training sample for
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
is an independent sample, Conditions
1
–
2
, and
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})=1
, it holds that for all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, each
t>0
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|(II)_{l,\kappa_{r}}|>\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\Big)&\leq\frac{24\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\mathbb{E}|m(\bm{X})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X})|^{3}\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|}{t\underline{\sigma}}\\
&\leq\frac{48\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}B_{1}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]}{t\underline{\sigma}}.\end{split}

(A.65)

By Markov’s inequality,
the assumption of i.i.d. observations, the assumption that the training sample for
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
is an independent sample, Condition
1
, and
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon)^{2}=1
, it holds that for all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, each
t>0
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|(III)_{l,\kappa_{r}}|>\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\Big)&\leq\frac{36B_{1}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}}{t\underline{\sigma}}.\end{split}

(A.66)

By Markov’s inequality, the assumption of i.i.d. observations, the assumption that the training sample for
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
is an independent sample, Jensen’s inequality, and Condition
1
, it holds that for all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, each
t>0
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\Big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|(IV)_{l,\kappa_{r}}|>\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\Big)&\leq\frac{24\sqrt{B_{1}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{3}\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{3}}{t\underline{\sigma}}.\end{split}

(A.67)

Next, we bound the term
\mathbb{P}\big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\widehat{\mu}_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})|>\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\big)
on the RHS of (
A.63
) by (
A.68
)–(
A.76
) below. For all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, each
t>0
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})-\widehat{\mu}_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})|\geq\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\big\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\widehat{\mu}_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\times\big[|\widehat{\mu}_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|+2|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\big]\geq\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\big\}\\
&=\mathbb{P}\big\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\widehat{\mu}_{l}(\kappa_{r})|^{2}+2|\widehat{\mu}_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\times|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\geq\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\big\}\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\widehat{\mu}_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\geq\sqrt{\frac{t\underline{\sigma}}{12}})+\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\widehat{\mu}_{l}(\kappa_{r})||\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\geq\frac{t\underline{\sigma}}{24})\\
&\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\widehat{\mu}_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\geq\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{t\underline{\sigma}}{12}},\frac{t\underline{\sigma}}{24\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\{1,|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\}}\right\}\right),\end{split}

(A.68)

where the first inequality follows from that
|x+y|\leq|x-y|+2|y|
. By the definition of
\widehat{\mu}_{l}(a)
and simple calculations, it holds that for every
t_{1}>0
, all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\widehat{\mu}_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\geq t_{1})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(V_{n}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{2}-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big\}\Big|\geq t_{1}),\end{split}

(A.69)

where
V_{n}\coloneqq n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i}))^{2}+2\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i})||\varepsilon_{i}|
.

To deal with the RHS of (
A.69
), define two events

\begin{split}A_{1}&=\cup_{i=1}^{n}\{|\varepsilon_{i}|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\},\\
A_{2r}&=\Big\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon_{i}|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\times\big[\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})\big\}\Big|\\
&\qquad\qquad>c_{1}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}\Big\},\end{split}

where
q_{1}=q-\beta
,
c_{1}=\sqrt{8}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}
, and

\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})=\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big\}.

Let us show that the probabilities of events
A_{1}
and
A_{2r}
are negligible in the following. By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, Markov’s inequality, and the model regularity assumptions (boundness of
\zeta(\cdot)
and that
\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{q\vee 8}<\infty
),

\begin{split}&\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|\\
&=\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\times\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big\}\\
&\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\mathbb{E}\big(\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\times\varepsilon^{2}\big)\\
&\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}(|\varepsilon|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}})}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4}\big)}\\
&\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq p}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon|^{q_{1}})}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4}\big)}\\
&=O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}),\end{split}

(A.70)

and hence for all large
n
and each
1\leq r\leq R
,

\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|\leq 1+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|<1+\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2}).

(A.71)

Now, by Markov’s inequality, Hoeffding’s inequality, (
A.71
), and model regularity assumptions, for all large
n
and each
1\leq r\leq R
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(A_{1})&\leq\big(n^{1-\frac{q}{q_{1}}}\big)\times\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{q}=o(1),\\
\mathbb{P}(A_{2r})&\leq 2p\exp{\left(-\frac{2c_{1}^{2}\times n^{1+\frac{4}{q_{1}}}\times\log{(n\vee p)}}{4n\big\{\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\times n^{\frac{2}{q_{1}}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|\big\}^{2}}\right)}=o(1).\end{split}

(A.72)

Moreover, let us deal with the term
V_{n}
on the RHS of (
A.69
). By Condition
1
, Jensen’s inequality,
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})=1
, and model regularity assumptions,

\mathbb{E}(V_{n})\leq B_{1}+2\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\sqrt{B_{1}}.

(A.73)

With the result (
A.72
),

{\small\begin{split}&\textnormal{RHS of \eqref{theorem4.3}}\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(\{V_{n}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{2}-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big\}\Big|\geq t_{1}\}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\cap A_{1}^{c}\cap A_{2r}^{c})+\mathbb{P}(A_{1}\cup A_{2r})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big\{V_{n}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon_{i}|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{2}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big\}\Big|\geq t_{1}\Big\}\cap A_{2r}^{c}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(A_{1}\cup A_{2r})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big\{V_{n}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon_{i}|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{2}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt-\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})\big\}\Big|\geq t_{1}\Big\}\cap A_{2r}^{c}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(A_{1}\cup A_{2r})\\
&\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}(V_{n})+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|+c_{1}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}}{t_{1}}+\mathbb{P}(A_{1}\cup A_{2r}).\end{split}}

(A.74)

By taking
t_{1}=\underline{\sigma}\times n^{-\beta_{2}}\times\big[24\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\{1,|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\}\big]^{-1}
in (
A.69
) and the results of (
A.70
) and (
A.74
), it holds that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\widehat{\mu}_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\geq t_{1})\\
&\leq c_{4}\Big[n^{\beta_{2}}\mathbb{E}(V_{n})+n^{\beta_{2}-\frac{1}{2}}+c_{1}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q-\beta}+\beta_{2}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}\Big]+\mathbb{P}(A_{1}\cup A_{2r}),\end{split}

(A.75)

where
c_{4}=24\times\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\{1,|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\}\times(\underline{\sigma})^{-1}
. By taking
t=n^{-\beta_{2}}
in (
A.68
) and the result of (
A.75
), it holds that for all large
n
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})-\widehat{\mu}_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})|\geq\frac{1}{6}n^{-\beta_{2}}\underline{\sigma})\\
&\leq 2c_{4}\Big[n^{\beta_{2}}\mathbb{E}(V_{n})+n^{\beta_{2}-\frac{1}{2}}+c_{1}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q-\beta}+\beta_{2}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}\Big]+2\mathbb{P}(A_{1}\cup A_{2r}),\end{split}

(A.76)

where we note that when
t=n^{-\beta_{2}}
, we have that for all large
n
,

\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{t\underline{\sigma}}{12}},\frac{t\underline{\sigma}}{24\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\{1,|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\}}\right\}=\frac{n^{-\beta_{2}}\underline{\sigma}}{24\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\{1,|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\}}.

Next, we show that
\mathbb{P}\big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|(V)_{l,\kappa_{r}}-\big[\sigma_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})+\mu_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})\big]\Big|>\frac{1}{6}t\underline{\sigma}\big)
on the RHS of (
A.63
) with
t=n^{-\beta_{2}}
is negligible. The arguments (
A.77
)–(
A.81
) below are quite similar to those for establishing the upper bound in (
A.76
). For each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
and
a\in\mathbb{R}
, define

\nu_{l}(a)=\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in(-\infty,a]}\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{4}\big\}=\sigma_{l}^{2}(a)+\mu_{l}^{2}(a).

In addition, for each
1\leq r\leq R
, define event

\begin{split}&A_{3r}=\Big\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon_{i}|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\times\big[\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}-\nu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})\big\}\Big|\\
&\qquad\qquad>c_{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{4}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}\Big\},\end{split}

where
q_{1}=q-\beta
,
\nu_{l}^{(n)}(a)=\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in(-\infty,a]}\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{4}\big\}
for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
and
a\in\mathbb{R}
, and
c_{3}=\sqrt{8}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}
. By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, Markov’s inequality, and the model regularity assumptions (boundness of
\zeta(\cdot)
and that
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon)^{8}<\infty
),

\begin{split}&\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\nu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\nu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|\\
&=\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{4}\big\}\\
&\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}\big(\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\varepsilon^{4}\big)\\
&\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}(|\varepsilon|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}})}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{8}\big)}\\
&\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq p}n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon|^{q_{1}})}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{8}\big)}\\
&=O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}),\end{split}

(A.77)

and hence for all large
n
and each
1\leq r\leq R
,

\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\nu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|\leq 1+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\nu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\leq 1+\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\times\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4}).

(A.78)

In addition, by Markov’s inequality, Hoeffding’s inequality, (
A.78
), and model regularity assumptions, for all large
n
and each
1\leq r\leq R
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(A_{3r})&\leq 2p\exp{\left(-\frac{2c_{3}^{2}n^{1+\frac{8}{q_{1}}}\log{(n\vee p)}}{4n\big\{\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\times n^{\frac{4}{q_{1}}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\nu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|\big\}^{2}}\right)}=o(1).\end{split}

(A.79)

By (
A.77
) and (
A.79
) and the definitions of
A_{1}
and
A_{3r}
, it holds that for each
t_{2}>0
, all
n\geq 1
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

{\small\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|(V)_{l,\kappa_{r}}-\big[\sigma_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})+\mu_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})\big]\Big|>t_{2}\big)\\
&=\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}-\nu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big\}|>t_{2})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}-\nu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big\}|>t_{2}\}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\cap A_{1}^{c}\cap A_{3r}^{c})+\mathbb{P}(A_{1}\cup A_{3r})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon_{i}|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt-\nu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big\}|>t_{2}\Big\}\cap A_{3r}^{c}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(A_{1}\cup A_{3r})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(c_{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{4}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\nu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\nu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|>t_{2})+\mathbb{P}(A_{1}\cup A_{3r})\\
&\leq\frac{c_{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{4}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\nu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\nu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|}{t_{2}}+\mathbb{P}(A_{1}\cup A_{3r}),\end{split}}

(A.80)

where we use Markov’s inequality in the last inequality.

With
t_{2}=\frac{1}{6}\underline{\sigma}n^{-\beta_{2}}
in (
A.80
), it holds that for all large
n
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, and each
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\big(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|(V)_{l,\kappa_{r}}-\big[\sigma_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})+\mu_{l}^{2}(\kappa_{r})\big]\Big|>n^{-\beta_{2}}\underline{\sigma}\frac{1}{6}\big)\\
&\leq 6(\underline{\sigma})^{-1}\times\Big[c_{3}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{4}{q-\beta}+\beta_{2}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}+n^{\beta_{2}-\frac{1}{2}}\Big]+\mathbb{P}(A_{1}\cup A_{3r}).\end{split}

(A.81)

Now, let us establish the upper bound for terms on the RHS of (
A.63
). With
t=n^{-\beta_{2}}
in (
A.63
),
(
A.64
)–(
A.67
), (
A.72
)–(
A.73
), (
A.76
), (
A.79
), (
A.81
),
0<\beta_{2}<\frac{1}{4}
, and the assumptions that
R
is a finite constant and that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}n^{\beta_{2}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}+\sqrt{n}B_{1}(-\log{B_{1}})+n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{4}{q-\beta}+\beta_{2}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}=0
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(|\sigma_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|>n^{-\beta_{2}})=0.

(A.82)

We note that
n^{\beta_{2}}\times\mathbb{E}(V_{n})
in (
A.76
) decreases to zero because we have assumed
\beta_{2}<\frac{1}{4}
as well as other regularity assumptions.

By (
A.58
)–(
A.60
), (
A.82
), and the assumption that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}B_{1}=0
we have finished the proof of (
A.53
).

A.5
Proof of Theorem
5

Let some
j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
such that
|\mu_{j}(a)|>\underline{\mu}
be given.
We begin with an application of Markov’s inequality as follows, where
\mathcal{A}=(-\infty,a]
with some break
a\in\mathbb{R}
. For each
n\geq 1
and
t>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}(a)\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(a)\big]^{-1}|\leq t)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)|\leq t|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(a)|+|A_{1}|+|A_{2}|)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(|\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(a)\big]|\leq|T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(a)\big]|+t|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(a)|+|A_{1}|+|A_{2}|)\\
&\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(a)\big]|)+t\mathbb{E}(|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(a)|)+\mathbb{E}(|A_{1}|)+\mathbb{E}(|A_{2}|)}{\sqrt{n}\underline{\mu}},\end{split}

(A.83)

where

\begin{split}A_{1}&=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}\big)(m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i}))^{2},\\
A_{2}&=2n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}\big)(m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i}))\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i},\\
T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)&=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}\big)(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{2},\end{split}

(A.84)

and the first inequality follows because
T_{j}(a)=A_{1}+A_{2}+T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)
.

Next, we establish upper bounds for terms on the RHS of (
A.83
). By arguments similar to those for (
A.11
),

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(a)\big]|)&\leq C\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})},\\
\mathbb{E}(|A_{2}|)&\leq 2C\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\sqrt{B_{1}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})},\end{split}

(A.85)

for some
C>0
, which is due to an application of the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality
(
Burkholder
et al. 1972
)
; note that for the second inequality in (
A.85
), it is required that the training sample for
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
is independent of
\{Y_{i},\bm{X}_{i},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{i},\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
.

By the assumptions that observations are i.i.d. and the training sample is an independent sample and Condition
1
,

\mathbb{E}(|A_{1}|)\leq\sqrt{n}B_{1}.

(A.86)

To deal with
\mathbb{E}(|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(a)|)
, we write

\begin{split}\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2}(a)&=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}})^{2}(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i})^{4}-\big[\widehat{\mu}_{j}(a)\big]^{2}\\
&\leq n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}})^{2}(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i})^{4}\\
&=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}})^{2}(\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i}))^{4}\\
&\qquad-4n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}})^{2}(\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i}))^{3}\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\\
&\qquad+6n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}})^{2}(\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i}))^{2}(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{2}\\
&\qquad-4n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}})^{2}(\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})-m(\bm{X}_{i}))(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{3}\\
&\qquad+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}})^{2}(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{4},\end{split}

(A.87)

where
\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i}=Y_{i}-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})
. Hence, by Jensen’s inequality, Conditions
1
–
2
,
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})<\infty
,
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})=1
in model (
1
), that
\zeta(\vec{z})
is positive, the assumptions that observations are i.i.d. and that
\varepsilon
is an independent model error, and the assumption that the training sample for
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
is an independent sample, for each
n\geq 1
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}(|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(a)|)\\
&\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2}(a)\big]}\\
&\leq\Big\{4\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}+8\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}B_{1}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\\
&\qquad+6B_{1}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}+4\sqrt{B_{1}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{3}\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{3}+\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(A.88)

By (
A.85
)–(
A.88
), the assumptions that
\lim\sup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}<\infty
,
B_{1}<1
, and that
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}\geq 1
, and the model regularity assumptions, there exists some
N>0
whose value is independent of
1\leq j\leq p
such that for all
n\geq N
and each
t>0
,

\begin{split}\textnormal{The RHS of \eqref{theorem5.1}}&\leq\frac{(1+t)\log{n}+\sqrt{n}B_{1}}{\sqrt{n}\underline{\mu}}\\
&\leq B_{1}(\underline{\mu})^{-1}+(\log{n})(1+t)(\sqrt{n}\underline{\mu})^{-1},\end{split}

which concludes the desired result of Theorem
5
for feature index
j
given at the beginning of this proof.

Lastly, we note that the generic constants such as
N>0
in this proof are not subject to feature index
j
. Therefore, we have finished the proof of Theorem
5
.

A.6
Proof of Theorem
6
