Let us begin with proving the first part of Theorem
6
, which is
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{l}\in S^{*})=1
when
\min_{l\in S^{*}}|\mu_{l}(a)|>\underline{\mu}>0
(
S^{*}
is not an empty set accordingly). Recall that
G_{l}(a)=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{U_{il}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{il}\in\mathcal{A}})(\widehat{\eta}_{i})^{2}
where
\widehat{\eta}_{i}=V_{i}-\widehat{m}(\bm{U}_{i})
,
\widehat{l}=\arg\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|G_{l}(a)|
, and
\mathcal{A}=(-\infty,a]
.

On the event

J_{1}=\left\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|G_{l}(a)-\mu_{l}(a)|\leq\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu}\right\},

it holds that

\begin{split}\min_{l\in S^{*}}|G_{l}(a)|&=\min_{l\in S^{*}}|G_{l}(a)-\mu_{l}(a)+\mu_{l}(a)|\\
&\geq\min_{l\in S^{*}}|\mu_{l}(a)|-\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|G_{l}(a)-\mu_{l}(a)|\\
&>\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|G_{l}(a)-\mu_{l}(a)|\\
&\geq\max_{l\not\in S^{*}}|G_{l}(a)|,\end{split}

where the second inequality is due to the assumption
\min_{l\in S^{*}}|\mu_{l}(a)|>\underline{\mu}
and the definition of
J_{1}
, and the third inequality follows from that
\mu_{l}(a)=0
if
l\not\in S^{*}
. This result shows that on
J_{1}
,

\widehat{l}\in S^{*}.

(A.89)

In the following, we show that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(J_{1})=1
, and begin with defining two events
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
with negligible probabilities. Define

\begin{split}E_{1}&=\cup_{i=1}^{n}\{|\eta_{i}|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\},\\
E_{2}&=\Big\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\eta_{i}|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\times\big[\bm{1}_{U_{il}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{il}\in\mathcal{A}}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\eta_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{l}^{(n)}\big\}\Big|>c_{1}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}\Big\},\end{split}

where
q_{1}=q-\beta
,
\mu_{l}^{(n)}=\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\eta|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in\mathcal{A}}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big\}
, and
c_{1}=\sqrt{8}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}
. By Jensen’s inequality, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, Markov’s inequality,
\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{q\vee 4}<\infty
, and other model regularity assumptions,

\begin{split}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(a)-\mu_{l}^{(n)}|&\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\mathbb{E}\big|\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in\mathcal{A}}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big|\\
&\leq\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\times\mathbb{E}\big(\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\varepsilon^{2}\big)\\
&\leq\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}(|\varepsilon|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}})}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4}\big)}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon|^{q_{1}})}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4}\big)}\\
&=O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}),\end{split}

(A.90)

and hence for all large
n
,

\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}^{(n)}|\leq 1+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(a)|\leq 1+\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2}).

(A.91)

By (
A.91
), Markov’s inequality, Hoeffding’s inequality,
\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{q\vee 4}<\infty
, and other model regularity assumptions, for all large
n
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(E_{1})&\leq\big(n^{1-\frac{q}{q_{1}}}\big)\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{q}=o(1),\\
\mathbb{P}(E_{2})&\leq 2p\times\exp{\left(-\frac{2c_{1}^{2}\times n^{-1+\frac{4}{q_{1}}}\times\log{(n\vee p)}}{4n\big\{\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}n^{\frac{2}{q_{1}}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}^{(n)}|\big\}^{2}}\right)}=o(1).\end{split}

(A.92)

With events
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
, we are ready to deal with
\mathbb{P}(J_{1})
. By simple calculations, the definitions of
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
, for each
t>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|G_{l}(a)-\mu_{l}(a)|>t)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(V_{n}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{U_{il}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{il}\in\mathcal{A}}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\eta_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{l}(a)\big\}|>t)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(\{V_{n}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{U_{il}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{il}\in\mathcal{A}}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\eta_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{l}(a)\big\}|>t\}\cap E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2}^{c})\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt+\mathbb{P}(E_{1}\cup E_{2})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(\{V_{n}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\eta_{i}|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\times\big[\bm{1}_{U_{il}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{il}\in\mathcal{A}}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\eta_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{l}(a)\big\}|>t\}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\cap E_{2}^{c})+\mathbb{P}(E_{1}\cup E_{2})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(V_{n}+c_{1}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(a)-\mu_{l}^{(n)}|>t)+\mathbb{P}(E_{1}\cup E_{2})\\
&\leq\frac{c_{1}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(a)-\mu_{l}^{(n)}|+\mathbb{E}(V_{n})}{t}+\mathbb{P}(E_{1}\cup E_{2}),\end{split}

(A.93)

where
V_{n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{U}_{i})-m(\bm{U}_{i})\big]^{2}+2|\widehat{m}(\bm{U}_{i})-m(\bm{U}_{i})|\times\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\times|\eta_{i}|\big\}
, and the last inequality is due to Markov’s inequality.

Next, we deal with
\mathbb{E}(V_{n})
. By Jensen’s inequality, Condition
1
,
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\underline{\mu}^{-1}\sqrt{B_{1}}=0
, the assumption of i.i.d. observations, the assumption Condition
4
that the training sample for constructing
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
is independent of
\{V_{i},\bm{U}_{i},\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i},\eta_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, and the assumptions that
\eta_{i}
’s are independent model errors and that
\eta_{1}
and
\varepsilon
have the same distribution,

\underline{\mu}^{-1}\mathbb{E}(V_{n})\leq\underline{\mu}^{-1}B_{1}+2\underline{\mu}^{-1}\sqrt{B_{1}}\times\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\times\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|=o(1).

(A.94)

By (
A.90
), (
A.92
)–(
A.94
) with
t=\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu}
, and the assumption that
\underline{\mu}^{-1}\times n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}=o(1)
(recall that
q_{1}=q-\beta
), we conclude that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c})=0,

(A.95)

which along with (
A.89
) proves the first part of Theorem
6
.

Let us proceed to show the second assertion of Theorem
6
.
By (
A.89
) and the assumption
\min_{l\in S^{*}}|\mu_{l}(a)|>\underline{\mu}
, it holds that on
J_{1}
,
\widehat{l}\in S^{*}
and
|\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)|\geq\underline{\mu}.
By this and Markov’s inequality, for each
t>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\big\{\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]^{-1}\big|\leq t\big\}\\
&=\mathbb{P}\big\{\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(a)-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)+T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]+\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big|\leq t\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big\}\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big\{\sqrt{n}\big|\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big|\leq t\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]+\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(a)-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)\big|+\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big|\big\}\cap J_{1}\Big\}\\
&\qquad+\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\big\{\underline{\mu}\sqrt{n}\leq t\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]+\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(a)-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)\big|+\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big|\big\}+\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c})\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\underline{\mu}}\left(t\mathbb{E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)|+\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(a)-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)\big|+\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big|\right)+\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c}).\end{split}

(A.96)

For the reader’s convenience,
T_{l}(a)
and
T_{l}^{(\star)}(a)
for
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
are given in (
A.84
), while an expression for
\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)
can be found in (
A.87
).

In what follows, we deal with the upper bounds for
\mathbb{E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)|
,
\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(a)-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)\big|
, and
\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big|
, and begin with the upper bound for
\mathbb{E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)|
. By Jensen’s inequality, Condition
4
, the assumption that
\varepsilon
is an independent model error with
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon)=0
and
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})=1
in model (
1
), and
Conditions
1
–
2
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)|\\
&\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}^{2}(a)|}\\
&\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1}^{4})}\\
&\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon+m(\bm{X})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X})\big]^{4}}\\
&\leq\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{4}+4\mathbb{E}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{3}\mathbb{E}|m(\bm{X})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X})|\\
&\qquad+6\mathbb{E}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\mathbb{E}(m(\bm{X})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}))^{2}+\mathbb{E}(m(\bm{X})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}))^{4}\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\leq\Big(\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})+4\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{3}\times\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{3}\times\sqrt{B_{1}}\\
&\qquad+6\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}B_{1}+4\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

(A.97)

where we use the result
\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2}(a)=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in\mathcal{A}})(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i})^{2}-\widehat{\mu}_{j}(a)\big\}^{2}\leq n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{i})^{4}
for each
j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
, the assumption of i.i.d. observations, and Condition
4
in the second inequality.

Next, we establish an upper bound for
\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big|
. Since
\widehat{l}
and
\widehat{a}_{l}
’s are independent of
T_{l}^{(\star)}(a)
’s,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big|&=\sum_{l=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\big\{\big|T_{l}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{l}(a)\big]\big|\bm{1}_{\widehat{l}=l}\big\}\\
&=\sum_{l=1}^{p}\big\{\mathbb{E}\big|T_{l}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{l}(a)\big]\big|\big\}\times\mathbb{P}(\widehat{l}=l).\end{split}

(A.98)

By the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality, Jensen’s inequality, and the assumption of i.i.d. observations, there exists some
K>0
such that for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
and each
n\geq 1
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\big|T_{l}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{l}(a)\big]\big|\\
&\leq K\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big\{\big(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,a]}\big)\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{l}(a)\big\}^{2}}\\
&\leq K\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}\big\}}\\
&\leq K\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})}.\end{split}

(A.99)

By (
A.98
)–(
A.99
), for each
n\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]\big|\leq K\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})},

(A.100)

where
K>0
is given in (
A.99
).

Next, we proceed to deal with the upper bound for
\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(a)-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)\big|
. By the definition of
T_{l}(a)
and
T_{l}^{(\star)}(a)
in (
A.84
), the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality, the arguments similar to those for (
A.60
),
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})=1
in model (
1
), there exists some
K>0
such that for each
n\geq 1
and
B_{1}>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(a)-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(a)\big|\\
&\leq 2\mathbb{E}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{i\widehat{l}}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{i\widehat{l}}\in(-\infty,a]}\big]\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\Big|\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbb{E}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}\\
&\leq 2K\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\sqrt{B_{1}}+\sqrt{n}B_{1}.\end{split}

(A.101)

By the model regularity assumptions, the assumption that
\lim\sup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}<\infty
, (
A.96
)–(
A.97
), and (
A.100
)–(
A.101
), it holds that for all large
n
, each
t>0
, and
0<B_{1}<1
,

\mathbb{P}\big\{\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(a)\big]^{-1}\big|\leq t\big\}\leq\frac{(1+t)\log{n}}{\sqrt{n}\underline{\mu}}+\frac{B_{1}}{\underline{\mu}}+\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c}),

(A.102)

which along with (
A.95
) and the assumption that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\underline{\mu}^{-1}\Big[\sqrt{B_{1}}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}\Big]=0

concludes the proof of Theorem
6
.

A.7
Proof of Theorem
7

Let some
j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
such that
\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{j}(\kappa_{r})|>\underline{\mu}
be given. We begin with an application of Markov’s inequality as follows. For each
n\geq 1
and
t>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]^{-1}|\leq t)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})|\leq t|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})|+|A_{1}(\widehat{a}_{j})|+|A_{2}(\widehat{a}_{j})|)\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\big\{\sqrt{n}\big|\mu_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|\leq\big|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]\big|+t|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})|+|A_{1}(\widehat{a}_{j})|+|A_{2}(\widehat{a}_{j})|\big\}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\cap J_{1}\bigg\}+\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\mu}}{3}\leq|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]|+t|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})|+|A_{1}(\widehat{a}_{j})|+|A_{2}(\widehat{a}_{j})|\right\}+\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c})\\
&\leq\frac{3}{\sqrt{n}\underline{\mu}}\times\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]\big|+t\times\mathbb{E}\big|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|+\mathbb{E}\big|A_{1}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|+\mathbb{E}\big|A_{2}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|\Big\}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt+\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c}),\end{split}

(A.103)

where

\begin{split}A_{1}(\widehat{a}_{j})&=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\widehat{a}_{j}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\widehat{a}_{j}]}\big)(m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i}))^{2},\\
A_{2}(\widehat{a}_{j})&=2n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\widehat{a}_{j}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\widehat{a}_{j}]}\big)(m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i}))\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i},\\
T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})&=n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{ij}\in(-\infty,\widehat{a}_{j}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\widehat{a}_{j}]}\big)(\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i})^{2},\\
J_{1}&\coloneqq\big\{|\mu_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})|>\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu}\big\},\end{split}

and the first inequality of (
A.103
) follows because
T_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})=A_{1}(\widehat{a}_{j})+A_{2}(\widehat{a}_{j})+T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})
.

In what follows, we establish upper bounds for terms on the RHS of (
A.103
), and begin with an upper bound for
\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c})
. We have

\big\{\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|G_{j}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{j}(\kappa_{r})|\leq\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu}\big\}\subset J_{1}

(A.104)

due to the assumption
\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{j}(\kappa_{r})|>\underline{\mu}
and that
\widehat{a}_{j}=\arg\max_{\kappa\in\{\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{R}\}}|G_{j}(\kappa)|
; here, for the reader’s convenience, recall that it is given in Section
2.3
(with
n_{2}=n
in this proof) that
G_{j}(\kappa_{r})=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{U_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]})(\widehat{\eta}_{i})^{2}
, where
\widehat{\eta}_{i}=V_{i}-\widehat{m}(\bm{U}_{i})
.

By (
A.104
), the definition of
G_{j}(\kappa_{r})
’s, and Markov’s inequality,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|G_{j}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{j}(\kappa_{r})|>\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu})\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(|G_{j}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{j}(\kappa_{r})|>\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu})\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{V_{n}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt+\Big|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\{\big[\bm{1}_{U_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\eta_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{j}(\kappa_{r})\bigg\}\Big|>\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu}\bigg\}\\
&\leq\frac{3}{\underline{\mu}}\bigg\{R\times\mathbb{E}(V_{n})\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\big[\bm{1}_{U_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\eta_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{j}(\kappa_{r})}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\bigg\},\end{split}

(A.105)

where
V_{n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{U}_{i})-m(\bm{U}_{i})\big]^{2}+2|\widehat{m}(\bm{U}_{i})-m(\bm{U}_{i})|\times\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\times|\eta_{i}|\big\}
.

Let us deal with the first term on the RHS of (
A.105
). By the assumptions that observations are i.i.d. and that
\eta_{i}
’s are independent model errors, Jensen’s inequality, Condition
4
, Condition
1
,
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})=1
in model (
1
), and other model regularity assumptions,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}(V_{n})\leq B_{1}+2\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\sqrt{B_{1}}.\end{split}

(A.106)

For the second term on the RHS of (
A.105
), we use the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality
(
Burkholder
et al. 1972
)
and model regularity assumptions to deduce that there exists some
K>0
such that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\big[\bm{1}_{U_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{ij}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\eta_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{j}(\kappa_{r})}{\sqrt{n}}\right|\leq K.\end{split}

(A.107)

By (
A.105
)–(
A.107
) and the assumption
B_{1}<1
, there exists
N_{1}>0
such that for all
n\geq N_{1}
,

\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c})\leq\frac{3R\sqrt{B_{1}}\big\{1+2\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\big\}+3RKn^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\underline{\mu}}.

(A.108)

Next, we proceed to establish upper bounds for terms
\mathbb{E}\big|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]\big|
,
\mathbb{E}\big|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|
,
\mathbb{E}\big|A_{1}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|
, and
\mathbb{E}\big|A_{2}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|
on the RHS of (
A.103
). By arguments similar to those for (
A.107
) and (
A.11
), there exists some
C>0
such that for each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(\kappa_{r})\big]\big|&\leq C\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})},\\
\mathbb{E}\big|A_{2}(\kappa_{r})\big|&\leq 2C\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\sqrt{B_{1}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})},\end{split}

(A.109)

where
C>0
is due to an application of the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality; note that for the second inequality in (
A.109
), it is required that the training sample for
\widehat{m}(\cdot)
is independent of
\{Y_{i},\bm{X}_{i},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{i},\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
.

By (
A.109
) and the assumption that
\widehat{a}_{j}
is independent of
T_{j}^{(\star)}
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]\big|&=\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{E}\big\{\big|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(\kappa_{r})\big]\big|\bm{1}_{\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r}}\big\}\\
&=\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{E}\big|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(\kappa_{r})\big]\big|\times\mathbb{P}(\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r})\\
&\leq C\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})},\end{split}

(A.110)

where
C
is given in (
A.109
). Similarly,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big|A_{2}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|&=\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{E}(|A_{2}(\kappa_{r})|\bm{1}_{\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r}})\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{E}|A_{2}(\kappa_{r})|\times\mathbb{P}(\widehat{a}_{j}=\kappa_{r})\\
&\leq 2C\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\sqrt{B_{1}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})}.\end{split}

(A.111)

Meanwhile, by the assumptions that observations are i.i.d., Condition
4
, and Condition
1
,

\mathbb{E}\big|A_{1}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|\leq\sqrt{n}B_{1}.

(A.112)

Next, we deal with the upper bound for
\mathbb{E}\big|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|
. By arguments similar to those for (
A.88
), for each
n\geq 1
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|&\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{j}^{2}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]}\\
&\leq\Big\{4\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}+8\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}B_{1}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\\
&\qquad+6B_{1}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}+4\sqrt{B_{1}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{3}\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{3}+\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(A.113)

By (
A.108
) with
K>0
given in (
A.107
), (
A.110
)–(
A.113
), Condition
2
with
\lim\sup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}<\infty
,
B_{1}<1
, and
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}\geq 1
, the assumption that
R
is finite, and other model regularity assumptions, there exists some
N_{2}>0
such that for all
n\geq N_{2}
and each
t>0
,

\begin{split}&\textnormal{The RHS of \eqref{theorem7.3}}\\
&\leq\frac{3\sqrt{n}B_{1}+3\mathbb{E}\big|T_{j}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{j})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]\big|+3t\times\mathbb{E}\big|\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|+3\mathbb{E}\big|A_{2}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big|}{\sqrt{n}\underline{\mu}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{3R\sqrt{B_{1}}\big\{1+2\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\big\}+3RKn^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{\underline{\mu}}\\
&\leq\underline{\mu}^{-1}\sqrt{B_{1}}\log{(n)}+(\log{n})(1+t)(\sqrt{n}\underline{\mu})^{-1},\end{split}

which concludes the desired result of Theorem
7
for feature index
j
given at the beginning of this proof.

Lastly, we note that the generic constants such as
C,K,N_{1},N_{2}
used in the proof are not subject to feature index
j
. Therefore, we have finished the proof of Theorem
7
.

A.8
Proof of Theorem
8

For the reader’s convenience, recall that for each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
,

\begin{split}G_{l}(\kappa_{r})&=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\bm{1}_{U_{il}\in(\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{il}\in(\infty,\kappa_{r}]})(\widehat{\eta}_{i})^{2},\\
\widehat{a}_{l}&=\arg\max_{\kappa\in\{\kappa_{1},\dots,\kappa_{R}\}}|G_{l}(\kappa)|,\\
\widehat{l}&=\arg\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|G_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})|,\end{split}

where
\widehat{\eta}_{i}=V_{i}-\widehat{m}(\bm{U}_{i})
.

Let us begin the formal proof with the first part of Theorem
8
, which is
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(\widehat{l}\in S^{*})=1
when
\min_{l\in S^{*}}\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|>\underline{\mu}>0
(
S^{*}
is not an empty set accordingly). On the event

J_{1}=\left\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\big||G_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})|-\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\big|\leq\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu}\right\},

it holds that

\begin{split}\min_{l\in S^{*}}|G_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})|&=\min_{l\in S^{*}}\Big[|G_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})|-\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|+\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\Big]\\
&\geq\min_{l\in S^{*}}\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|-\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big||G_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})|-\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\Big|\\
&\geq\frac{2}{3}\underline{\mu}\\
&>\max_{l\not\in S^{*}}|G_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})|,\end{split}

where the second inequality follows from the assumption
\min_{l\in S^{*}}\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|>\underline{\mu}
and the definition of
J_{1}
, and the third inequality follows from that
\max_{l\not\in S^{*}}\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|=0
and the definition of
J_{1}
. This result shows that on
J_{1}
,

\widehat{l}\in S^{*}.

(A.114)

In the following, we conclude the first part of Theorem
8
by proving that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c})=0.
On the event

J_{2}\coloneqq\Big\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p,1\leq r\leq R}|G_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\leq\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu}\Big\},

it holds that for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
,

\begin{split}|G_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})|&=\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|G_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})+\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\\
&\geq\max_{1\leq r\leq R}\Big\{|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|-|G_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\Big\}\\
&\geq\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|-\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu},\end{split}

(A.115)

where the first inequality follows from the definition of
\widehat{a}_{l}
in Section
2.4
, and similarly,

\begin{split}|G_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})|&=\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|G_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})+\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\\
&\leq\max_{1\leq r\leq R}\Big\{|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|+|G_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\Big\}\\
&\leq\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|+\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu}.\end{split}

Therefore,

J_{2}\subset J_{1},

(A.116)

and hence
\mathbb{P}(J_{1}^{c})\leq\mathbb{P}(J_{2}^{c})
. Next, let us show that
\mathbb{P}(J_{2}^{c})
is negligible as follows.

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(J_{2}^{c})\\
&=\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq p,1\leq r\leq R}|G_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|>\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu})\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|G_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|>\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu})\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}\Big(V_{n}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{\big[\bm{1}_{U_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\eta_{i}\big]^{2}\\
&\qquad-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\Big\}\Big|>\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu}\Big),\\
\end{split}

(A.117)

where
V_{n}=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\big[\widehat{m}(\bm{U}_{i})-m(\bm{U}_{i})\big]^{2}+2|\widehat{m}(\bm{U}_{i})-m(\bm{U}_{i})|\times\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\times|\eta_{i}|\big\}
, and the second inequality follows because
V_{n}
is not subject to feature index
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
. To deal with the RHS of (
A.117
), we define the following events.

\begin{split}&E_{1}=\cup_{i=1}^{n}\{|\eta_{i}|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\},\\
&E_{2r}=\Big\{\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\Big|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\eta_{i}|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\times\big[\bm{1}_{U_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\eta_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})\big\}\Big|\\
&\qquad\qquad>c_{1}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}\Big\},\textnormal{ for }r=1,\dots,R,\end{split}

where
q_{1}=q-\beta
,
\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})=\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\eta|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big\}
, and
c_{1}=\sqrt{8}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}
. In (
A.119
) below, we show that events
E_{1}
and
E_{2r}
’s are negligible; first, we need (
A.118
) below. By the Jensen’s inequality, Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, Markov’s inequality,
\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{q\vee 4}<\infty
, and other model regularity assumptions,

\begin{split}&\max_{r\in\{1,\dots,R\}}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|\\
&\leq\max_{r\in\{1,\dots,R\}}\max_{1\leq l\leq p}\mathbb{E}\big|\bm{1}_{|\varepsilon|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{l}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big|\\
&\leq\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\varepsilon|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\varepsilon^{2}\big\}\\
&\leq\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}(|\varepsilon|>n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}})}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4}\big)}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon|^{q_{1}})}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4}\big)}\\
&=O(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}).\end{split}

(A.118)

By (
A.118
), it holds that for all large
n
and each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
,

\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|\leq 1+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|\leq 1+\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2}).

By this, Markov’s inequality, Hoeffding’s inequality,
\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{q\vee 4}<\infty
, and other model regularity assumptions, for all large
n
and each
1\leq r\leq R
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(E_{1})&\leq\big(n^{1-\frac{q}{q_{1}}}\big)\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{q}=o(1),\\
\mathbb{P}(E_{2r})&\leq 2p\times\exp{\left(\frac{-2c_{1}^{2}\times n^{-1+\frac{4}{q_{1}}}\times\log{(n\vee p)}}{4n\big\{\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}n^{\frac{2}{q_{1}}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|\big\}^{2}}\right)}=o(1).\end{split}

(A.119)

With events
E_{1}
and
E_{2r}
, we are ready to deal with the RHS of (
A.117
). By Markov’s inequality and the definitions of
E_{1}
and
E_{2r}
, for each
t>0
,

\begin{split}&\textnormal{RHS of \eqref{theorem8.3}}\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(A_{1r}\cap E_{1}^{c}\cap E_{2r}^{c})+\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(E_{1}\cup E_{2r})\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(A_{2r}\cap E_{2r}^{c})+\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(E_{1}\cup E_{2r})\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(V_{n}+c_{1}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|>\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu})\\
&\qquad+\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(E_{1}\cup E_{2r})\\
&\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\frac{3}{\underline{\mu}}\left[c_{1}n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})-\mu_{l}^{(n)}(\kappa_{r})|+\mathbb{E}(V_{n})\right]\\
&\qquad+\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(E_{1}\cup E_{2r}),\end{split}

(A.120)

where
V_{n}
is defined in (
A.117
) and

\begin{split}A_{1r}&=\{V_{n}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{U_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\eta_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big\}|>\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu}\},\\
A_{2r}&=\{V_{n}+\max_{1\leq l\leq p}|n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{|\eta_{i}|\leq n^{\frac{1}{q_{1}}}\}}\times\big[\bm{1}_{U_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{U}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{U}_{iS^{*}})\eta_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big\}|>\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu}\},\end{split}

and the last inequality is due to Markov’s inequality; here, events
A_{1r}
’s and
A_{2r}
’s are defined and used only to simplify the expressions in (
A.120
).

In addition, by Jensen’s inequality, Condition
1
, the assumptions that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\underline{\mu}^{-1}\sqrt{B_{1}}=0
and observations are i.i.d., the assumptions that
\eta_{i}
’s are independent model errors and that
\eta_{1}
and
\varepsilon
have the same distribution,
Condition
4
, and other model regularity assumptions,

\underline{\mu}^{-1}\mathbb{E}(V_{n})\leq\underline{\mu}^{-1}B_{1}+2\underline{\mu}^{-1}\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\sqrt{B_{1}}\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|=o(1).

(A.121)

By (
A.117
)–(
A.121
), and the assumptions that
R
is finite and that
\underline{\mu}^{-1}\times n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}=o(1)
(recall that
q_{1}=q-\beta
), we conclude that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(J_{2}^{c})=0,

(A.122)

which along with (
A.114
) and (
A.116
) proves the first part of Theorem
8
.

Next, we show the second assertion of Theorem
8
.
By (
A.115
), for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
, on
J_{2}
,

\begin{split}|\mu_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})|&=|\mu_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})-G_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})+G_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})|\\
&\geq|G_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})|-|\mu_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})-G_{l}(\widehat{a}_{l})|\\
&\geq\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|-\frac{2}{3}\underline{\mu},\end{split}

(A.123)

which in combination with (
A.114
), (
A.116
), and the assumption
\min_{l\in S^{*}}\max_{1\leq r\leq R}|\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})|>\underline{\mu}
shows that on
J_{2}
, it holds that
\widehat{l}\in S^{*}
and
|\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|\geq\frac{1}{3}\underline{\mu}.
By this and Markov’s inequality, for each
t>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\big\{\big|\big[T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]^{-1}\big|\leq t\big\}\\
&=\mathbb{P}\big\{\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})+T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]+\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big|\leq t\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big\}\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\Big\{\big\{\sqrt{n}\big|\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big|\leq t\times\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]+\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big|\\
&\qquad+\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big|\big\}\cap J_{2}\Big\}+\mathbb{P}(J_{2}^{c})\\
&\leq\mathbb{P}\big\{\frac{\underline{\mu}\sqrt{n}}{3}\leq t\times\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]+\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big|+\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big|\big\}\\
&\qquad+\mathbb{P}(J_{2}^{c})\\
&\leq\frac{3}{\sqrt{n}\underline{\mu}}\left(t\times\mathbb{E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|+\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big|+\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big|\right)\\
&\qquad+\mathbb{P}(J_{2}^{c}).\end{split}

(A.124)

In what follows, we establish upper bounds for terms
\mathbb{E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|
,
\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big|
, and
\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big|
on the RHS of (
A.124
), and begin with the one for
\mathbb{E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|
.

By Jensen’s inequality,
the assumption of i.i.d. observations, Condition
4
, the assumption that
\varepsilon
is an independent model error with
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon)=0
and
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})=1
, and
Conditions
1
–
2
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|\\
&\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}|\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})|^{2}}\\
&\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{1})^{4}}\\
&\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon+m(\bm{X})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X})\big]^{4}}\\
&\leq\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{4}+4\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{3}\big|m(\bm{X})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X})\big|\big\}\\
&\qquad+6\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big|m(\bm{X})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X})\big|^{2}\big\}+4\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]\big|m(\bm{X})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X})\big|^{3}\big\}\\
&\qquad+\mathbb{E}(m(\bm{X})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}))^{4}\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\leq\Big\{\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})+4\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{3}\mathbb{E}|\varepsilon|^{3}\sqrt{B_{1}}\\
&\qquad\qquad+6\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}B_{1}+4\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}\Big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(A.125)

Next, we establish an upper bound for
\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big|
. Since
\widehat{l}
and
\widehat{a}_{l}
’s are independent of
T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})
’s,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big|\\
&=\sum_{l=1}^{p}\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{E}\big\{\big|T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})-\sqrt{n}\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big|\bm{1}_{\{\widehat{l}=l\}\cap\{\widehat{a}_{l}=\kappa_{r}\}}\big\}\\
&=\sum_{l=1}^{p}\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{E}\big|T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})-\sqrt{n}\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big|\times\mathbb{P}(\{\widehat{l}=l\}\cap\{\widehat{a}_{l}=\kappa_{r}\}).\end{split}

(A.126)

By the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality, Jensen’s inequality, and the assumption of i.i.d. observations, there exists some
K>0
such that for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
, each
1\leq r\leq R
, and each
n\geq 1
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\big|T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big]\big|\\
&\leq K\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big\{\big(\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big)\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{2}-\mu_{l}(\kappa_{r})\big\}^{2}}\\
&\leq K\sqrt{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\big]^{4}\big\}}\\
&\leq K\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})}.\end{split}

(A.127)

By (
A.126
)–(
A.127
) and that
\sum_{l=1}^{p}\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(\{\widehat{l}=l\}\cap\{\widehat{a}_{l}=\kappa_{r}\})=1
, for each
n\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-\sqrt{n}\big[\mu_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big|\leq K\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})},

(A.128)

where
K>0
is given in (
A.127
).

Next, we establish an upper bound for
\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big|
. Since
\widehat{l}
and
\widehat{a}_{l}
’s are independent of
T_{l}(\kappa_{r})
’s and
T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})
’s,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big|&=\sum_{l=1}^{p}\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{E}\big\{\big|T_{l}(\kappa_{r})-T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})\big|\bm{1}_{\{\widehat{l}=l\}\cap\{\widehat{a}_{l}=\kappa_{r}\}}\big\}\\
&=\sum_{l=1}^{p}\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{E}\big|T_{l}(\kappa_{r})-T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})\big|\times\mathbb{P}(\{\widehat{l}=l\}\cap\{\widehat{a}_{l}=\kappa_{r}\}).\end{split}

(A.129)

By the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality, Jensen’s inequality, Condition
1
, Condition
4
, the assumption that observations are i.i.d., model assumption (
1
), and other model regularity assumptions, there exists some
K>0
such that for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
and each
r\in\{1,\dots,R\}
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\big|T_{l}(\kappa_{r})-T_{l}^{(\star)}(\kappa_{r})\big|\\
&\leq 2\mathbb{E}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{il}\in(-\infty,\kappa_{r}]}\big]\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\zeta(\bm{X}_{iS^{*}})\varepsilon_{i}\Big|\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[m(\bm{X}_{i})-\widehat{m}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]^{2}\\
&\leq 2K\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\sqrt{B_{1}}+\sqrt{n}B_{1},\end{split}

which in combination with (
A.129
) and the fact that
\sum_{l=1}^{p}\sum_{r=1}^{R}\mathbb{P}(\{\widehat{l}=l\}\cap\{\widehat{a}_{l}=\kappa_{r}\})=1
leads to

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big|T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})-T_{\widehat{l}}^{(\star)}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big|\leq 2K\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\sqrt{B_{1}}+\sqrt{n}B_{1}.\end{split}

(A.130)

By the model regularity assumptions, the assumption that
\lim\sup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muM\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{n}^{2}B_{1}<\infty
, (
A.124
)–(
A.125
), (
A.128
), and (
A.130
), it holds that for all large
n
, each
t>0
, and each
0<B_{1}<1
,

\mathbb{P}\big\{\big|\big[T_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{\widehat{l}}(\widehat{a}_{\widehat{l}})\big]^{-1}\big|\leq t\big\}\leq\frac{(1+t)\log{n}}{\sqrt{n}\underline{\mu}}+\frac{3B_{1}}{\underline{\mu}}+\mathbb{P}(J_{2}^{c}),

(A.131)

which along with (
A.122
) and the assumption that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\underline{\mu}^{-1}\Big[\sqrt{B_{1}}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2}{q_{1}}}\sqrt{\log{(n\vee p)}}\Big]=0
concludes the proof of Theorem
8
.

Appendix B
Proofs of Lemma
1
and Example
5

B.1
Proof of Lemma
1

The proof for the case with
S^{*}=\emptyset
is trivial since we have defined in Section
1.1
that
\zeta(\bm{X}_{\emptyset})=\zeta_{0}
. Therefore, we consider the case with
S^{*}\not=\emptyset
and that
X_{j}
is a null feature; that is,
j\not\in S^{*}
in model (
1
). In what follows, we show that
(\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}),\bm{X}_{-j},X_{j})
and
(\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}),\bm{X}_{-j},\widetilde{X}_{j})
have the same distribution, which implies the desired result of Lemma
1
. It follows from Definition
1
of coordinate-wise knockoffs and the definition of model (
1
) that
\widetilde{X}_{j}
is independent of
\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})
conditional on
\bm{X}
. In addition,
X_{j}
is independent of
\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})
conditional on
\bm{X}_{-j}
.

For every
\mathcal{A}_{1}\in\mathcal{R}
,
\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\mathcal{R}^{p-1}
,
\mathcal{A}_{3}\in\mathcal{R}
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\{(\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}),\bm{X}_{-j},X_{j})\in(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3})\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{P}\{(\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}),\bm{X}_{-j},X_{j})\in(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3})|\bm{X}_{-j}\}\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\{\bm{1}_{\{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}\}}\mathbb{P}\{(\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}),X_{j})\in(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{3})|\bm{X}_{-j}\}\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\{\bm{1}_{\{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}\}}\mathbb{P}\{\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\in\mathcal{A}_{1}|\bm{X}_{-j}\}\mathbb{P}\{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{3}|\bm{X}_{-j}\}\},\end{split}

(A.132)

and

\mathbb{P}\{\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\in\mathcal{A}_{1}|\bm{X}_{-j}\}=\mathbb{P}\{\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\in\mathcal{A}_{1}|\bm{X}_{-j},X_{j}\},

(A.133)

where the last equality in (
A.132
) and (
A.133
) follow because
X_{j}
is independent of
\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})
conditional on
\bm{X}_{-j}
.

Next,

\begin{split}&\textnormal{RHS of \eqref{km.3}}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\{\bm{1}_{\{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}\}}\mathbb{P}\{\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\in\mathcal{A}_{1}|\bm{X}_{-j}\}\mathbb{P}\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{3}|\bm{X}_{-j}\}\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\{\bm{1}_{\{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}\}}\mathbb{P}\{\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\in\mathcal{A}_{1}|\bm{X}_{-j}\}\mathbb{P}\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{3}|\bm{X}_{-j},X_{j}\}\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\{\bm{1}_{\{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}\}}\mathbb{P}\{\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\in\mathcal{A}_{1}|\bm{X}_{-j},X_{j}\}\mathbb{P}\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{3}|\bm{X}_{-j},X_{j}\}\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\{\bm{1}_{\{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{A}_{2}\}}\mathbb{P}\{(\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}),\widetilde{X}_{j})\in(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{3})|\bm{X}_{-j},X_{j}\}\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\{\mathbb{P}\{(\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}),\bm{X}_{-j},\widetilde{X}_{j})\in(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3})|\bm{X}_{-j},X_{j}\}\}\\
&=\mathbb{P}\{(\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}),\bm{X}_{-j},\widetilde{X}_{j})\in(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3})\},\end{split}

(A.134)

where the first equality follows from the definition of knockoff features in Definition
1
, the second equality is an application of law of total expectation, the third equality is from (
A.133
), and the fourth equality follows because
\widetilde{X}_{j}
is independent of
\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})
conditional on
\bm{X}
.

By (
A.132
) and (
A.134
), we have that for every
\mathcal{A}_{1}\in\mathcal{R}
,
\mathcal{A}_{2}\in\mathcal{R}^{p-1}
,
\mathcal{A}_{3}\in\mathcal{R}
,

\mathbb{P}\{(\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}),\bm{X}_{-j},X_{j})\in(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3})\}=\mathbb{P}\{(\varepsilon\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}),\bm{X}_{-j},\widetilde{X}_{j})\in(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathcal{A}_{3})\},

which along with an application of the
\pi-\lambda
Theorem
(
Durrett 2019
)
concludes the desired result of Lemma
1
. We omit the details of the application of the
\pi-\lambda
Theorem for simplicity.

B.2
Proof of Example
5

Lemma
2
in Section
C.1
is needed for the proof of Example
5
. To use Lemma
2
to prove Example
5
, we first show that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\leq\delta\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}>1-\delta\}}|\bm{X}_{-j})&>\delta^{2}(\bar{c})^{-1}\underline{c}\qquad\textnormal{ almost surely,}\\
\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}>1-\delta\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\leq\delta\}}|\bm{X}_{-j})&>\delta^{2}(\bar{c})^{-1}\underline{c}\qquad\textnormal{ almost surely,}\end{split}

(A.135)

and we begin with the first inequality in (
A.135
).

By the distributional assumption of Example
5
, it holds that for every Borel set
\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{R}^{p-1}
with
|\mathcal{C}|_{L}>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\leq\delta\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}>1-\delta\}}|\bm{X}_{-j})\bm{1}_{\{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{C}\}}\big\}&=\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\leq\delta\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}>1-\delta\}}\bm{1}_{\{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{C}\}}\big\}\\
&>|\mathcal{C}|_{L}\delta^{2}\underline{c}.\end{split}

(A.136)

Consider a Borel set
\mathcal{C}^{\star}=\{\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\leq\delta\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}>1-\delta\}}|\bm{X}_{-j})\leq\delta^{2}(\bar{c})^{-1}\underline{c}\}
. By the definition of
\mathcal{C}^{\star}
and the distributional assumption, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq\delta}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}>1-\delta\}}|\bm{X}_{-j})\bm{1}_{\{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{C}^{\star}\}}\big\}\leq\delta^{2}\underline{c}\times|\mathcal{C}^{\star}|_{L},\end{split}

(A.137)

which along with (
A.136
) and the distributional assumption concludes that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{C}^{\star})=0
, and hence the proof of the first inequality of (
A.135
). The other result in (
A.135
) holds by the same arguments, and we omit the details for simplicity.

Now, by Lemma
2
and (
A.135
), we set
w_{0}
in Lemma
2
to
\delta^{2}(\bar{c})^{-1}\underline{c}
to finish the proof of Example
5
.

Appendix C
Additional technical proofs and supplementary material

C.1
Lemma
2
and its proof

Without loss of generality, let
S^{*}=\{1,\dots,s^{*}\}
for some integer
s^{*}\geq 1
. Recall that we have defined
\zeta_{j}(x)
to be
\zeta(z_{1},\dots,z_{j-1},x,z_{j+1}\dots,z_{p})
for
\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
; if
s^{*}=1
, then
\zeta_{j}(x)=\zeta(x)
and that
\zeta_{j}(x)
is invariant to
\vec{z}
.

Lemma 2
.

Assume
\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})>0
,
\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})<\infty
, and that for every
j\in S^{*}=\{1,\dots,s^{*}\}
and every
\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}
,
\zeta_{j}(x)
is nondecreasing (or nonincreasing) in
x
. In addition, let some constants
0<\delta<\frac{1}{2},\iota>0,w_{0}>0
, and Cartesian product
\mathcal{D}\in\mathcal{R}^{s^{*}}
be given such that
1)

\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq\delta}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}>1-\delta\}}|\bm{X}_{-j})>w_{0}
and
\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}>1-\delta\}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\leq\delta}|\bm{X}_{-j})>w_{0}
, and
2)
for every
j\in S^{*}
and every
\vec{z}\in\mathcal{D}
,
\big|\zeta_{j}(1-\delta)-\zeta_{j}(\delta)\big|>\iota
. Then,
\min_{j\in S^{*}}\inf_{\delta<\kappa<1-\delta}|\mu_{j}(\kappa)|>\iota w_{0}\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D})\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]
.

Proof of Lemma
2
Let any
\delta<\kappa<1-\delta
and
j\in S^{*}
be given. Since
\mathcal{D}
is a Cartesian product, we have
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{1}\times\dots\times\mathcal{D}_{s^{*}}
for some
\mathcal{D}_{l}\in\mathcal{R}
for each
l\in\{1,\dots,s^{*}\}
. By the assumptions on
\zeta(\cdot)
, it holds that for every
\vec{z}\in\mathcal{D}
,

\iota<|\zeta_{j}(1-\delta)-\zeta_{j}(\delta)|\leq|\zeta_{j}(1-\delta)-\zeta_{j}(\kappa)|+|\zeta_{j}(\kappa)-\zeta_{j}(\delta)|.

(A.138)

For the case with
s^{*}>1
, we define sets
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\star}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\star}
as follows. By (
A.138
), we can write
\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\star}\cup\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\star}
such that 1)
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\star}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\star}
are Borel rectangles satisfying (
A.144
) in Section
C.2
with the
j
th coordinate as
\mathcal{D}_{j}
and 2)
|\zeta_{j}(\kappa)-\zeta_{j}(\delta)|>\frac{1}{2}\iota
for every
\vec{z}\in\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\star}
and
|\zeta_{j}(1-\delta)-\zeta_{j}(\kappa)|>\frac{1}{2}\iota
for every
\vec{z}\in\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\star}
. On the other hand, for case with
s^{*}=1
, define
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\star}=\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\star}=\mathcal{D}_{1}
(note that
S^{*}=\{1\}
here).

By the construction of
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\star}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\star}
, one of (
A.139
) and (
A.140
) below holds.

\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\star})\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D}),

(A.139)

\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\star})\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D}).

(A.140)

Note that if (
A.139
) does not hold, then (
A.140
) is true; it is possible they both hold.

Let us consider the case with (
A.139
) first, and we will use Lemma
3
with
q_{1}=\delta
and
q_{2}=\kappa
to finish the proof, where
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
are parameters in Lemma
3
. By the choice of
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
, for every
\vec{z}\in\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\star}
,

|\zeta_{j}(q_{1})-\zeta_{j}(q_{2})|>\frac{1}{2}\iota.

(A.141)

In addition, since
\delta\leq q_{1}<q_{2}\leq\kappa<1-\delta
in this scenario, by the assumptions of Lemma
2
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq q_{1}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}>\kappa}|\bm{X}_{-j})\geq\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq\delta}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}>1-\delta}|\bm{X}_{-j})>w_{0},\\
\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{X_{j}>q_{2}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\leq\kappa}|\bm{X}_{-j})\geq\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{X_{j}>1-\delta}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\leq\delta}|\bm{X}_{-j})>w_{0}.\end{split}

(A.142)

By (
A.139
), (
A.141
)–(
A.142
), and the definition of
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\star}
, we use Lemma
3
in Section
C.2
to conclude

|\mu_{j}(\kappa)|>\frac{1}{4}\iota w_{0}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D})\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big],

(A.143)

which holds for every
\delta<\kappa<1-\delta
and
j\in S^{*}
. By (
A.143
), we conclude Lemma
2
for the case with (
A.139
).

For the other case with (
A.140
), we may set
q_{1}=\kappa
and
q_{2}=1-\delta
for the subsequent arguments, whose details are omitted because they are similar to those for (
A.143
). We have completed the proof of Lemma
2
.

C.2
Lemma
3
and its proof

In this section, we state Lemma
3
, which is a general version of Lemma
2
, and its proof.
Let
\zeta_{j}(x)
be defined as in Section
C.1
.
To facilitate the analysis in Lemma
3
, consider some
\mathcal{D}^{(j)}\in\mathcal{R}^{s^{*}}
satisfying the following condition: if
s^{*}>1
, then there exist some
\mathcal{A}\in\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{B}\in\mathcal{R}^{s^{*}-1}
such that

\mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{B}=\{(z_{j},\underbrace{z_{1},\dots,z_{j-1},z_{j+1},\dots,z_{s^{*}}}_{\textnormal{without the }j\textnormal{th coordinate}}):\vec{z}\in\mathcal{D}^{(j)}\},

(A.144)

where the notation
\times
denotes the Cartesian product operation; if
s^{*}=1
, there is no additional restriction on
\mathcal{D}^{(j)}\in\mathcal{R}
. Cartesian product
\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\dots\times\mathcal{A}_{s^{*}}
with
\mathcal{A}_{l}\in\mathcal{R}
for each
l\in\{1,\dots,s^{*}\}
is an example of Borel rectangles satisfying (
A.144
).

Lemma 3
.

Assume
\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})>0
and
\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})<\infty
. Let any
j\in S^{*}=\{1,\dots,s^{*}\}
,
w_{0}>0
,
\iota>0
,
q_{1}<q_{2}
,
\mathcal{D}^{(j)}\in\mathcal{R}^{s^{*}}
satisfying (
A.144
), and
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
be given such that the following conditions are satisfied.
1)

q_{1}<q_{2}\leq\kappa
or
\kappa\leq q_{1}<q_{2}
.
2)

\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq q_{1}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in(-\infty,\kappa]}|\bm{X}_{-j})>w_{0}
and
\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{X_{j}>q_{2}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in(-\infty,\kappa]^{c}}|\bm{X}_{-j})>w_{0}
.
3)
\zeta_{j}(x)
is nondecreasing (or nonincreasing) in
x
for every
\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}
.
4)

\big|\zeta_{j}(q_{1})-\zeta_{j}(q_{2})\big|>\iota
for every
\vec{z}\in\mathcal{D}^{(j)}
. Then
|\mu_{j}(\kappa)|>\iota w_{0}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D}^{(j)})\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]
.

Proof of Lemma
3
To emphasize the
j
th coordinate, we denote
\zeta(X_{j},\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})=\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})
with a slight abuse of notation; if
s^{*}=1
, then
\zeta(X_{j},\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})=\zeta(X_{j})
. As a result, assumption 3) of Lemma
3
is equivalent to that
\zeta(x,\vec{z}_{-j})
is nondecreasing (or nonincreasing) in
x
for every
\vec{z}=(z_{1},\dots,z_{s^{*}})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}
, where
\vec{z}_{-j}=(z_{1},\dots,z_{j-1},z_{j+1},\dots,z_{s^{*}})^{\top}
. Let us deal with the case with a nondecreasing
\zeta_{j}(x)
and
q_{1}<q_{2}\leq\kappa
first.

Let
\mathcal{A}=(-\infty,\kappa]
. By the assumptions that
\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})>0
,
\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})<\infty
, and Var
(\varepsilon)=1
in model (
1
), it holds that both
\zeta^{2}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})
and
\varepsilon^{2}
are integrable. Then, by the assumption that
\varepsilon
is independent of
\bm{X}
and that

\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}=\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}},

it holds that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\Big\{\varepsilon^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}-\varepsilon^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\Big\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}-\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\Big\}.\end{split}

(A.145)

By the assumptions on
\mathcal{D}^{(j)}
, if
s^{*}>1
, we let
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{D}_{-j}\times\mathbb{R}^{p-s^{*}}
, where

\mathcal{D}_{j}\times\mathcal{D}_{-j}=\{(z_{j},\underbrace{z_{1},\dots,z_{j-1},z_{j+1},\dots,z_{s^{*}}}_{\textnormal{without the }j\textnormal{th coordinate}}):\vec{z}\in\mathcal{D}^{(j)}\}

for some
\mathcal{D}_{j}\in\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{D}_{-j}\in\mathcal{R}^{s^{*}-1}
; if
s^{*}=1
, we let
\mathcal{B}=\mathbb{R}^{p-1}
. By the definitions of
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{D}_{-j}
, it holds that

\begin{cases}\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{B})=\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}}\in\mathcal{D}_{-j})\geq\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D}^{(j)})\textnormal{ if }s^{*}>1,\\
\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{B})=1\geq\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D}^{(j)}),\textnormal{ o.w.}\end{cases}

(A.146)

By the monotonicity assumption on
\zeta_{j}(x)
and
\kappa=\sup\mathcal{A}
,

\begin{split}&\textnormal{RHS of \eqref{power.31} }\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}-\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\Big\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\\
&\qquad\qquad-\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\big|\bm{X}_{-j}\Big\}\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{B}}\Big\}\\
&+\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\\
&\qquad\qquad-\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\big|\bm{X}_{-j}\Big\}\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\not\in\mathcal{B}}\Big\}.\end{split}

(A.147)

Let us deal with the first term on the RHS of (
A.147
). Recall that

\zeta(x,\vec{z}_{-j})=\zeta_{j}(x)
for each
x\in\mathbb{R}
and every
\vec{z}\in\mathcal{D}^{(j)}
if
s^{*}>1
, while
\zeta(x,\vec{z}_{-j})=\zeta_{j}(x)=\zeta(x)
if
s^{*}=1
. By assumption 4) of Lemma
3
, (
A.148
) below gives a lower bound of
\big[\zeta(q_{2},\vec{z}_{-j})\big]^{2}-\big[\zeta(q_{1},\vec{z}_{-j})\big]^{2}
in terms of
\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})
. For every
\vec{z}\in\mathcal{D}^{(j)}
,

\begin{split}\big[\zeta(q_{2},\vec{z}_{-j})\big]^{2}-\big[\zeta(q_{1},\vec{z}_{-j})\big]^{2}&=\big[\zeta(q_{2},\vec{z}_{-j})-\zeta(q_{1},\vec{z}_{-j})\big]\big[\zeta(q_{2},\vec{z}_{-j})+\zeta(q_{1},\vec{z}_{-j})\big]\\
&>\iota\big[\zeta(q_{2},\vec{z}_{-j})+\zeta(q_{1},\vec{z}_{-j})\big]\\
&\geq\iota\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z}).\end{split}

(A.148)

By (
A.148
),
q_{1}<q_{2}\leq\kappa=\sup\mathcal{A}
in this scenario, and the monotonicity assumption on
\zeta_{j}(x)
, on
\{\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D}^{(j)}\}
,

\begin{split}&\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\\
&\leq\big[\zeta(q_{1},\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq q_{1}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}+\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}\}\cap\{X_{j}>q_{1}\}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\\
&<\Big\{-\iota\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]+\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\Big\}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq q_{1}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\\
&\qquad+\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}\}\cap\{X_{j}>q_{1}\}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\\
&=-\iota\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq q_{1}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}+\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}.\end{split}

(A.149)

In addition, by the definition of knockoffs in Definition
1
, it holds that
\mathbb{P}(X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}|\bm{X}_{-j})=\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}|\bm{X}_{-j})
. Therefore,

\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}\}\cap\{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}\}}-\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}\}}|\bm{X}_{-j})=\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}|\bm{X}_{-j})=0.

(A.150)

By this and (
A.149
),

\begin{split}&\textnormal{The first term on the RHS of \eqref{power.34}}\\
&<-\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbb{E}\big\{\iota\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq q_{1}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}|\bm{X}_{-j}\big\}\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{B}}\big\}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt+\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big\{\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\Big(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\Big)\big|\bm{X}_{-j}\Big\}\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{B}}\Big\}\\
&=-\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbb{E}\big\{\iota\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq q_{1}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}|\bm{X}_{-j}\big\}\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{B}}\big\}\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt+\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\big|\bm{X}_{-j}\Big)\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{B}}\Big\}\\
&=-\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\big\{\mathbb{E}\big\{\iota\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq q_{1}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}|\bm{X}_{-j}\big\}\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{B}}\big\},\end{split}

(A.151)

where in the last equality, we use (
A.150
).

Next, we proceed to deal with the second term on the RHS of (
A.147
). By
\kappa=\sup\mathcal{A}
, assumption 3) of Lemma
3
, and (
A.150
),

\begin{split}&\textnormal{The second term on the RHS of \eqref{power.34}}\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big(\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\\
&\qquad-\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\big|\bm{X}_{-j}\Big)\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\not\in\mathcal{B}}\Big)\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big(\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\Big(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\Big)\big|\bm{X}_{-j}\Big)\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\not\in\mathcal{B}}\Big)\\
&=\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}\big|\bm{X}_{-j}\Big)\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\not\in\mathcal{B}}\Big)=0.\end{split}

(A.152)

By (
A.146
) and (
A.151
)–(
A.152
), the assumptions
\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq q_{1}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}|\bm{X}_{-j})>w_{0}
,
\textnormal{Var}(\varepsilon)=1
, and
\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})>0
,

\begin{split}\textnormal{The RHS of \eqref{power.34}}&<-\iota\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\leq q_{1}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}}|\bm{X}_{-j})\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{B}}\big]\\
&<-\iota w_{0}\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D}^{(j)}),\end{split}

(A.153)

leading to the desired result of Lemma
3
for the case assuming a nondecreasing
\zeta_{j}(x)
and
q_{1}<q_{2}\leq\kappa
.

For the case assuming a nondecreasing
\zeta_{j}(x)
and
\kappa\leq q_{1}<q_{2}
, we first note that

-\mu_{j}(\kappa)=\mathbb{E}\Big\{\varepsilon^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}-\varepsilon^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\Big\},

(A.154)

since
\bm{1}_{x\in\mathcal{A}}=1-\bm{1}_{x\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}
, where
\mathcal{A}^{c}
denotes the complementary event of
\mathcal{A}
. Therefore, the desired result can be obtained by considering event
\mathcal{A}^{c}
in place of
\mathcal{A}
. In light of this observation and an argument similar to that for (
A.147
) but with
\sup\mathcal{A}=\kappa\leq q_{1}<q_{2}
, we write

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\Big(\varepsilon^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}-\varepsilon^{2}\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\Big)\\
&\geq\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\Big(\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}-\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\Big)\\
&=\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big(\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad-\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\big|\bm{X}_{-j}\Big)\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{B}}\Big)\\
&\ \ +\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big(\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad-\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\bm{1}_{X_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}\big|\bm{X}_{-j}\Big)\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\not\in\mathcal{B}}\Big).\end{split}

(A.155)

Following the arguments for (
A.152
), the second term on the RHS of (
A.155
) is nonnegative. For the first term on the RHS of (
A.155
) in this scenario, by (
A.148
), on
\{\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D}^{(j)}\}
,

\begin{split}&\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}}\\
&=\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\big]^{2}\big[\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}>q_{2}\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}}+\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}\cap\{X_{j}\leq q_{2}\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}}\big]\\
&\geq\big[\zeta(q_{2},\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}>q_{2}\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}}+\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}\cap\{X_{j}\leq q_{2}\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}}\\
&>\Big(\iota\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]+\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\Big)\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}>q_{2}\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}}+\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}\cap\{X_{j}\leq q_{2}\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}}\\
&=\iota\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}>q_{2}\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}}+\big[\zeta(\kappa,\bm{X}_{S^{*}\backslash\{j\}})\big]^{2}\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}}\bm{1}_{\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}\}}.\end{split}

With these results, the assumption that
\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{X_{j}>q_{2}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}|\bm{X}_{-j})>w_{0}
, and similar arguments for (
A.151
), we deduce that

\begin{split}\textnormal{The RHS of \eqref{power.40}}&>\iota\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}(\bm{1}_{X_{j}>q_{2}}\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\not\in\mathcal{A}^{c}}|\bm{X}_{-j})\bm{1}_{\bm{X}_{-j}\in\mathcal{B}}\big]\\
&>\iota w_{0}\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]\mathbb{P}(\bm{X}_{S^{*}}\in\mathcal{D}^{(j)}),\end{split}

(A.156)

which along with (
A.154
) concludes the proof of this case assuming a nondecreasing
\zeta_{j}(x)
and
\kappa\leq q_{1}<q_{2}
.

We omit the proof for the cases assuming that
\zeta_{j}(x)
is nonincreasing because the proof is quite similar to the one here. We have completed the proof of Lemma
3
.

C.3
Lemma
4
and its proof

All notation is the same as in model (
1
) and Section
3
. Particularly, recall that for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
and
a\in\mathbb{R}
,

\begin{split}\sigma_{j}^{2}(a)&=\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big[\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,a]}\big]-\mu_{j}(a)\big\}^{2},\\
\mu_{j}(a)&=\mathbb{E}\big\{\big[\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,a]}\big]\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big\},\end{split}

where
\bm{X}
is the population
p
-dimensional feature vector,
\widetilde{\bm{X}}=(\widetilde{X}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{X}_{p})^{\top}
is the
p
-dimensional coordinate-wise knockoff feature vector of
\bm{X}
defined in Section
2.1
,
\varepsilon
is the independent model error,
\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})
is the standard deviation function defined in model (
1
), and
s^{*}=|S^{*}|
is the number of relevant features. In addition, we have defined
\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})=\zeta_{0}
for some
\zeta_{0}>0
if
S^{*}=\emptyset
in Section
1.1
. Note that Lemma
4
below does not assume
\mu_{j}(a)=0
.

Lemma 4
.

Let some constants
c>0
and
a\in\mathbb{R}
be given. Assume
\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})>0
,
\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})<\infty
,
\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})<\infty
, and that for each
j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\big\{\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,a]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,a]\}\big\}&\geq c,\\
\mathbb{P}\big\{\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,a]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(a,\infty)\}\big\}&\geq c.\end{split}

(A.157)

Then

\begin{split}&\min_{1\leq j\leq p}\sigma_{j}^{2}(a)\\
&\geq\min\left\{\frac{c}{2}\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4}),\frac{c^{3}\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{8}\big[\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})\big]^{2}}{16\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\big[\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\big]^{2}}\right\}\eqqcolon\underline{\sigma}^{2}>0.\end{split}

(A.158)

Proof of Lemma
4
By (
A.157
) and the assumption that
\varepsilon
is independent of
(\bm{X},\widetilde{\bm{X}})
, it holds that for each
1\leq j\leq p
,

\begin{split}\sigma_{j}^{2}(a)&\geq\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,a]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(a,\infty)\}}\big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,a]}\big)-\mu_{j}(a)\big\}^{2}\big\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,a]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(a,\infty)\}}\big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}-\mu_{j}(a)\big\}^{2}\big\}\\
&\geq c\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})-2|\mu_{j}(a)|\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2},\\
\sigma_{j}^{2}(a)&\geq\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,a]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,a]\}}\big\{\big[\zeta(\bm{X}_{S^{*}})\varepsilon\big]^{2}\big(\bm{1}_{X_{j}\in(-\infty,a]}-\bm{1}_{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,a]}\big)-\mu_{j}(a)\big\}^{2}\big\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\big\{\bm{1}_{\{X_{j}\in(-\infty,a]\}\cap\{\widetilde{X}_{j}\in(-\infty,a]\}}\big[\mu_{j}(a)\big]^{2}\big\}\\
&\geq c\big[\mu_{j}(a)\big]^{2}.\end{split}

By this, we deduce that if

2|\mu_{j}(a)|\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\leq\frac{c}{2}\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4}),

then for each
j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
,

\sigma_{j}^{2}(a)\geq\frac{c}{2}\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4}).

Otherwise, we have
|\mu_{j}(a)|>c\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})\big\{4\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{2}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\big\}^{-1}
, and hence for each
j\in\{1,\dots,p\}
,

\begin{split}\sigma_{j}^{2}(a)\geq\frac{c^{3}\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{8}\big[\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})\big]^{2}}{16\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\big[\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\big]^{2}}.\end{split}

Therefore, we conclude that

\min_{1\leq j\leq p}\sigma_{j}^{2}(a)\geq\min\left\{\frac{c}{2}\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4}),\frac{c^{3}\big[\inf_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{8}\big[\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{4})\big]^{2}}{16\big[\sup_{\vec{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{s^{*}}}\zeta(\vec{z})\big]^{4}\big[\mathbb{E}(\varepsilon^{2})\big]^{2}}\right\}>0,

which is the desired result.

Appendix D
Extensive simulation studies

Simulation experiments for the VD and VDBP tests with synthetic data are in Section
D.1
, where we demonstrate satisfactory empirical performance of these tests when the mean estimation methods (the centering methods) are random forests and HBART
(
Pratola et al. 2020
)
. HBART is also considered here since it is a recent advanced heteroskedasticity modeling method. For our synthetic data study, the benchmark methods are DGLM
(
Smyth 1989
)
and Two-hit
(
Chiou
et al. 2020
)
. On the other hand, some numerical experiments for hidden Markov model knockoffs
(
Serfling 2009
)
are in Section
D.2
.

D.1
Synthetic data study

D.1.1
Coordinate-wise Gaussian knockoffs

We have implemented a knockoff generator for producing approximate coordinate-wise Gaussian knockoffs in a coordinate-wise fashion based on the ideas of (
4
) and those introduced in
(
Barber and
Candès 2019
)
. To demonstrate the advantage of coordinate-wise knockoffs, we have performed a numerical experiment to show that the correlation between a Gaussian variable and its coordinate-wise knockoff tend to be smaller, sometimes much smaller, than the correlation between the Gaussian variable and its knockoff generated by the R package
knockoff

(
Barber and
Candès 2019
)
. Details of our coordinate-wise Gaussian knockoff generator and the results of our numerical experiments are postponed to Section
E
.

D.1.2
Simulation setting

We simulate a sample
\mathcal{X}_{n}\coloneqq\{Q_{i},\bm{Z}_{i},\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
of i.i.d. observations with
n\in\{500,700\}
such that
(Q_{1},\bm{Z}_{1})
and
(Y,\bm{X})
have the same distribution given as follows. The response
Y
is generated from one of the following models:

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle=X_{1}+X_{2}+\Big[\sqrt{\exp{(0.5+X_{10}+X_{15})}}\Big]\varepsilon,

(A.159)

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle=2X_{1}X_{2}+X_{3}+X_{4}+X_{5}^{2}+\Big[\sqrt{\exp{(0.5+X_{10}+X_{15})}}\Big]\varepsilon,

(A.160)

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle=2X_{1}X_{2}+X_{3}+X_{4}+X_{5}^{2}+\varepsilon,

(A.161)

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle=2X_{1}X_{2}+X_{3}+X_{4}+X_{5}^{2}+\Big[1+3\times\bm{1}_{\{X_{15}>0\}}\Big]\varepsilon,

(A.162)

where
\varepsilon
is an independent standard Gaussian model error, and
\bm{X}=(X_{1},\dots,X_{p})^{\top}
with
p\in\{20,700\}
is either 1) a multivariate Gaussian vector with zero mean and covariance matrix
\Sigma=[\Sigma_{lk}]_{l,k=1}^{p}
, in which
\Sigma_{lk}=\rho^{|l-k|}
, or 2) a multivariate t distribution with zero mean, covariance matrix
\Sigma
, and degree of freedom
10
. In practice, we use the R package
mvtnorm
for sampling
\{\bm{Z}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
from these multivariate distributions, while their knockoff features
\{\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are generated by our coordinate-wise Gaussian knockoff generator introduced in Section
E
with
\{\bm{Z}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
given. Note that existing knockoff generators, including ours in Section
E
, are all applicable for producing knockoffs
\{\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
given an arbitrary sample
\{\bm{Z}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, but whether
\{\widetilde{\bm{Z}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are good approximations of the ideal coordinate-wise knockoffs depends on the underlying distribution of the sample.

D.1.3
VD test for hypothesis (
2
)

The VD test statistic with break selection
\big|T_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big[\widehat{\sigma}_{j}(\widehat{a}_{j})\big]^{-1}\big|
given in (
12
) is calculated and the test is established for each
j\in\{1,\dots,20\}
. The empirical rejection rates over
100
repetitions of each simulation case are reported without adjusting for multiple tests in Tables
3
–
4
, where the rejection thresholds under null hypothesis (
2
) with test size
\alpha\in\{0.1,0.05,0.025\}
are given respectively by
(t_{0.1},t_{0.05},t_{0.025})=(1.64,1.96,2.25)
for the VD test, according to Theorem
3
. The multiplicity adjustment is not made in this simulation experiment because the goal here is to illustrate our main results in Theorem
3
. In Tables
3
–
4
, only two features
X_{10}
and
X_{15}
indicated with checkmarks are relevant, which means that entries for other features in the tables are empirical wrong rejection rates. Each simulation setting including the underlying feature index
j\in\{1,\dots,20\}
, values of
(n,p,\rho)
, test size
\alpha\in\{0.1,0.05,0.025\}
, data generating models (
A.159
)–(
A.160
), and test methods is indicated for each case and each panel in the tables. In addition, the centering methods are indicated on the top of each panel; they are random forests
(
Breiman 2001
)
and HBART
(
Pratola et al. 2020
)
, which are implemented with the R packages
randomForest
and
rbart
, respectively. In the simulation of the VD test, the sample features
\{\bm{X}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are sampled from the multivariate Gaussian distribution with covariance matrix
\Sigma
(see Section
D.1.2
for details). In Table
3
, we also consider heuristic P-values calculated by double GLM
(
Smyth 1989
;
Sesia
et al. 2019
)
with the R package
dglm
for each
j\in\{1,\dots p\}
. We do not report results from DGLM in Table
4
because
dglm
does not output heuristic P-values for the non-linear high-dimensional case.

The VD test with break selection requires sample splitting and a choice of break candidates, which we introduce as follows. The simulated sample
\mathcal{X}_{n}
(see Section
D.1.2
) is split into two subsamples
\mathcal{X}_{1}=\{Y_{i},\bm{X}_{i},\widetilde{\bm{X}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n_{1}}
and
\mathcal{X}_{2}=\{V_{i},\bm{U}_{i},\widetilde{\bm{U}}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n_{2}}
with
n_{1}=\lfloor\frac{2}{3}n\rceil
and
n_{2}=n-n_{1}
. As have mentioned in Section
2.4
, sample
\mathcal{X}_{1}
is used for constructing test statistics, while
\mathcal{X}_{2}
is used for selecting breaks. Our practical implementation of the VD test uses the full sample
\mathcal{X}_{n}
for training the regression trees estimate of mean functions for simplicity.
In addition, for each
l\in\{1,\dots,p\}
, we set break candidates
(\kappa_{1,l},\kappa_{R,l})
to respectively the first and third quartiles of
(U_{1l},\dots,U_{n_{2}l}
) with
R=100
,
\kappa_{r,l}<\kappa_{r+1,l}
, and evenly distributed
\kappa_{r,l}
’s.
